adam/creation/cover

The Creation of Adam

native illustration · openValley
adam/creation/p0

Before Adam, there were the skies, the earth, the angels, and everything around them. But there was no human being yet. Then Allah decided to create a human being, and He told the angels: “Indeed, I am going to create a human being from clay” (Qur’an 38:71).

painting · sideStreet
adam/creation/p1

So Allah shaped Adam from clay, and when He finished giving him a good shape, He breathed into him from His spirit. Allah said: “And when I have proportioned him and breathed into him of My spirit, then fall down to him in prostration” (Qur’an 38:72). At that moment the very first human being came to life.

native illustration · reedScreen
adam/creation/p2

The Qur’an tells us that Allah “began the creation of humankind from clay” (Qur’an 32:8) and that He “perfected everything that He created” (Qur’an 32:7). Adam was never an accident — he was shaped slowly, carefully, and beautifully by his Lord.

native illustration · terracedSlope
adam/creation/p3

After creating Adam, Allah told the angels: “Indeed, I will place upon the earth a successor” (Qur’an 2:30). The angels asked why Allah would place people there who would cause trouble and shed blood, but Allah answered: “Indeed, I know that which you do not know” (Qur’an 2:30).

native illustration · timberFrame
adam/creation/p4

Adam’s creation is a sign and a gift at the same time. The same Allah who shaped the first man from dust can create anything, and He gave Adam — and every child after him — a wonderful beginning.

native illustration · colonnade
adam/names/cover

The Knowledge of the Names

native illustration · sunkenCourt
adam/names/p0

Adam had something very special: Allah taught him the names of everything. The Qur’an says: “And He taught Adam the names, all of them” (Qur’an 2:31). Nothing in the world was hidden from him, because Allah had shared some of His own knowledge with him.

native illustration · windowSeat
adam/names/p1

To show how great this gift was, Allah showed those things to the angels and asked them: “Tell Me the names of these, if you are truthful?” (Qur’an 2:31). But the angels, even though they are noble and pure, replied: “Glory be to You! We have no knowledge except what You have taught us” (Qur’an 2:32).

native illustration · steppedPlatform
adam/names/p2

Then Allah said: “O Adam, inform them of their names” (Qur’an 2:33). And Adam told them every name — the names of everything Allah had placed before them.

native illustration · stoneTerrace
adam/names/p3

This was a great honor for Adam. It also teaches us something lovely: learning is a gift from Allah. Every time we learn a new word, we are using a little bit of the wonderful gift Allah gave to the very first human being.

native illustration · mosaicFloor
adam/angels-bow/cover

Angels Bow, Iblis Refuses

native illustration · palmGrove
adam/angels-bow/p0

After the angels saw Adam name everything, Allah asked them to bow down their heads before Adam — not to worship him, but to honor what Allah had created. Allah said: “And when We said to the angels: prostrate yourselves to Adam, and they prostrated, except Iblis” (Qur’an 2:34).

native illustration · stoneBridge
adam/angels-bow/p1

Every single angel obeyed that command. But Iblis refused. He said: “You created me from fire, and You created him from clay — I am better than him” (Qur’an 38:76). Because of his pride, he would not follow Allah’s order.

native illustration · courtyard
adam/angels-bow/p2

Pride was Iblis’s biggest mistake. He thought he was better than Adam just because he came from fire and Adam came from clay. So Allah said: “Depart from here, for you are rejected” (Qur’an 38:78).

native illustration · rockGorge
adam/angels-bow/p3

Iblis asked Allah to keep him alive until the Day of Judgment, and Allah allowed it. Then Iblis made a promise: “I will surely lead Your servants astray” — but he had to admit: “except for Your chosen servants among them” (Qur’an 38:82–83).

native illustration · sandyBasin
adam/angels-bow/p4

This is why Adam is so special: Allah chose him and honored him. And it teaches us how dangerous pride is. The moment we start thinking we are better than others, we should remember how pride sent Iblis away from Allah’s mercy.

native illustration · roofTerrace
adam/paradise/cover

Life in Paradise

native illustration · gardenWall
adam/paradise/p0

Allah said to Adam: “O Adam, dwell you and your wife in the Garden, and eat from wherever you wish” (Qur’an 2:35). So Adam and Hawwa (Eve) lived in the beautiful Garden Allah had created — a place with no hunger, no cold, and no fear.

native illustration · roundWindow
adam/paradise/p1

In Paradise, Adam never felt thirsty, never got uncomfortably hot, and never knew worry. Allah promised: “Truly it is for you that you will not be hungry therein or be unclothed, and that you will not be thirsty therein or be harmed by the heat of the sun” (Qur’an 20:118–119).

native illustration · canopy
adam/paradise/p2

Allah gave Adam and his wife only one simple rule: “Do not approach this tree, or you will be among the wrongdoers” (Qur’an 2:35). They could eat anything else — but this one tree was a test, a gentle reminder to obey their Creator.

native illustration · archway
adam/paradise/p3

So the first days of humankind were peaceful. Adam and Hawaa had everything, and all that was asked of them was to obey the one command from the One who gave them the Garden.

native illustration · cliffLedge
adam/test/cover

The Test of the Tree

native illustration · alcove
adam/test/p0

Iblis was waiting for his chance. He came to Adam and his wife and said: “O Adam, shall I lead you to the tree of eternity and a kingdom that never decays?” (Qur’an 20:120). He whispered softly to make them forget the one simple rule.

native illustration · wovenMat
adam/test/p1

In a moment of weakness, Adam and his wife both forgot the command. The Qur’an says: “Adam disobeyed his Lord, and he became confused with misguidance” (Qur’an 20:121).

native illustration · shelf
adam/test/p2

At once they felt ashamed. Their bodies exposed, and they began to cover themselves with leaves from the Garden (Qur’an 20:121). They understood that they had done something wrong against their kind Lord.

native illustration · windingPath
adam/test/p3

Adam and Hawaa learned an important lesson: Iblis is a clear enemy of human beings. This is why Allah warns us again and again in the Qur’an (Qur’an 2:208). And in the same way Shaytan came to Adam and his wife, taking them away from their Lord.

native illustration · raisedDais
adam/test/p4

This chapter teaches us that even the best human being can slip. What matters after a mistake is what we do next — and Adam will show us in the final chapters how to come back to Allah.

native illustration · riverBend
adam/descent/cover

Coming Down to Earth

native illustration · gateway
adam/descent/p0

After the slip, Allah said to Adam and his wife: “Get down from here, some of you are enemies of others” (Qur’an 2:38). So Adam and Hawaa left the Garden and came to the earth — the home we all live in today.

native illustration · clothBackdrop
adam/descent/p1

It must have felt strange and sad to leave such a beautiful Garden. But Allah did not leave them without comfort. Allah taught them the way back: “Whoever follows My guidance, no fear shall be upon them, nor will they grieve” (Qur’an 2:38).

native illustration · hillTown
adam/descent/p2

That is the great promise: the earth would not be random. Allah was sending guidance — prophets, books, and the knowledge of what Allah loves — so that every human being could stay safe, and so that Adam’s children could never be truly lost.

native illustration · recess
adam/descent/p3

Adam and Hawaa began their new life on the earth. They became the parents of all people, a human family that remembers a beginning, and learns the important lesson that our true home is forever with Allah.

native illustration · openValley
adam/repentance/cover

Adam Repents, and Allah Forgives

native illustration · sideStreet
adam/repentance/p0

Adam felt very sorry for what he had done. So he and his wife called from their hearts with a beautiful prayer: “Our Lord! We have wronged ourselves, and if You do not forgive us and have mercy upon us, we will surely be among the losers” (Qur’an 7:23).

native illustration · reedScreen
adam/repentance/p1

Allah answered their prayer. The Qur’an says: “Then Adam received from his Lord words of forgiveness, and He turned to him with mercy. Indeed, He is the Ever-Returning, the Most Merciful” (Qur’an 2:37).

native illustration · terracedSlope
adam/repentance/p2

Allah did not just forgive Adam — He chose him and guided him. The Word of Allah continues: “Then his Lord chose him, and He turned to him with forgiveness, and He guided him” (Qur’an 20:122). So Adam, whose slip looked so big, became one of the noble chosen prophets of Allah.

native illustration · timberFrame
adam/repentance/p3

Adam taught his children the most important lesson for when we make mistakes: admit it, say that we have done wrong, and return to Allah from the heart. Allah, the Most Merciful, is always ready to forgive those who truly turn back to Him.

native illustration · colonnade
adam/first-prophet/cover

Adam, the First Prophet

native illustration · sunkenCourt
adam/first-prophet/p0

Adam is called the father of all human beings. He is also honored as the very first prophet — the first person Allah chose to lead his family and the people after him toward the worship of Allah alone.

native illustration · windowSeat
adam/first-prophet/p1

Adam carried the message with a gentle heart. Allah taught him everything he needed to live on the earth, to grow food, to speak kindly, and to care for the children Allah gave him.

native illustration · steppedPlatform
adam/first-prophet/p2

Adam and Hawaa raised their family on earth. Their children learned from them that everyone is equal, and that we all come from one mother and one father. That is why the Qur’an reminds us: “O mankind, indeed We created you from a male and a female and made you into nations and tribes, so that you may know one another” (Qur’an 49:13).

native illustration · stoneTerrace
adam/first-prophet/p3

Prophet Muhammad, peace and blessings be upon him, reminded us how great Adam is. Adam is the very first human being, the first prophet, and the father of all later prophets. His story — from the honored clay, to the Garden, to the slip, and to the mercy of Allah — is the doorway through which all the other prophets followed.

native illustration · mosaicFloor
adam/first-prophet/p4

So when we look at the world Allah made, we can remember Adam’s story. His life whispers: do not fall into the pride of Iblis, and whenever you slip, the mercy of Allah is bigger than any mistake.

native illustration · palmGrove
idris/truthful-prophet/cover

A Truthful Prophet

native illustration · colonnade
idris/truthful-prophet/p0

Long ago, Allah sent a special messenger named Idris to guide his people. The Qur’an tells us about him in just a few beautiful words: “And mention in the Book Idris. Indeed, he was a man of truth and a prophet” (Qur’an 19:56).

native illustration · sunkenCourt
idris/truthful-prophet/p1

Idris always spoke the truth. Whether he was talking to his family, his friends, or even people who disagreed with him, his words were always honest. The people of his time knew that if the Prophet Idris said something, it was true.

native illustration · windowSeat
idris/truthful-prophet/p2

He was also very patient. Being a prophet is not easy — people often make fun of the messengers of Allah and refuse to listen. But Idris kept inviting people to goodness, gently and kindly, and he never gave up.

native illustration · steppedPlatform
idris/truthful-prophet/p3

Truth and patience were the treasures of Idris. He held onto both, and both made him a beloved and honored servant of Allah.

native illustration · stoneTerrace
idris/gift-of-knowledge/cover

The Gift of Knowledge

native illustration · mosaicFloor
idris/gift-of-knowledge/p0

Allah also gave Idris a very special gift: knowledge. Allah taught him many things, and Idris used what he learned to teach his people what is good and what is not good.

native illustration · palmGrove
idris/gift-of-knowledge/p1

Idris did not keep his knowledge to himself. He taught people about the laws of Allah and about how to live fairly and kindly with one another. He is remembered as a great teacher among the early prophets.

native illustration · stoneBridge
idris/gift-of-knowledge/p2

The people around Idris learned from his words, but even more from his actions. When they saw him being honest, gentle and patient every single day, they could see with their own eyes what truth really looks like in a person’s life.

native illustration · courtyard
idris/gift-of-knowledge/p3

Knowledge is one of the greatest gifts of Allah. He gave it to Idris, and it helped Idris become a light for his people. When we learn good things and use them to help others, we are doing something very much like what Idris did.

native illustration · rockGorge
idris/raised-high/cover

Raised to a High Station

native illustration · sandyBasin
idris/raised-high/p0

Nothing in the world is worth as much as being close to Allah. And for Idris, Allah gave a very special honor — the Qur’an tells us He raised him to a lofty place (Qur’an 19:56).

native illustration · roofTerrace
idris/raised-high/p1

Some scholars explain that Idris was taken up and raised to a station of great honor in the heavens. The beloved Prophet Muhammad ﷺ met him on his journey through the heavens, where Idris was waiting for him among the honored prophets.

native illustration · gardenWall
idris/raised-high/p2

Allah raised Idris because of his truthfulness and his patience. It was not his power or his riches that lifted him up — it was the pure heart he kept for his Lord, a heart that held onto the truth and never let it go.

native illustration · roundWindow
idris/raised-high/p3

For every child, this is a beautiful promise: people who are truthful and patient are lifted up by Allah and kept close to the ones He honors.

native illustration · canopy
idris/the-lesson/cover

The Lesson of Idris

native illustration · archway
idris/the-lesson/p0

The story of Idris is short, but it carries two big lessons. The first lesson: a truthful person is honored by Allah. The second lesson: a patient person is raised up high.

native illustration · cliffLedge
idris/the-lesson/p1

Sometimes telling the truth is hard, and being patient is even harder. But the life of Idris shows us that these struggles are never wasted. A person who stays truthful in the hard moments and patient in the difficult days is loved by Allah.

native illustration · alcove
idris/the-lesson/p2

Never give up. Idris never gave up, even when his people were slow to listen. There will always be something difficult — at school, at home, or inside ourselves — but a patient and honest heart keeps moving forward, and Allah is with it.

native illustration · wovenMat
idris/the-lesson/p3

Tell the truth, be patient, learn good things, and always trust in Allah. That is the lamp of Idris, and if we hold it, it will lead us close to the ones Allah loves.

native illustration · shelf
idris/legacy-and-lesson/cover

Legacy and Lesson

native illustration · windingPath
idris/legacy-and-lesson/p0

After the Prophet ﷺ died, the companions carried what they had learned outward — to new towns, to their children, and to whoever asked how to pray, give, or forgive.

native illustration · raisedDais
idris/legacy-and-lesson/p1

Idris's legacy is not only a story to remember. It is a way of acting that is still available: truthful speech, patient hardship, generous hands.

native illustration · riverBend
idris/legacy-and-lesson/p2

Close this part with one question: which single choice from this story could you copy this week — at home, at school, or with a friend?

native illustration · gateway
nuh/the-call/cover

The Call of Nuh

native illustration · mosaicFloor
nuh/the-call/p0

Allah sent Nuh to his people, who had forgotten their Creator and worshipped statues made of wood and stone. The Qur'an tells us what Nuh said: "O my people, worship Allah — you have no god other than Him. Indeed, I fear for you the punishment of a great Day" (Qur'an 11:25–26).

native illustration · palmGrove
nuh/the-call/p1

Nuh did not give up after one day, or one year, or one generation. The Qur'an says plainly: "He remained among them a thousand years minus fifty years" (Qur'an 29:14). For 950 years he called his people — morning and evening, in secret and in public.

native illustration · stoneBridge
nuh/the-call/p2

Allah preserved Nuh's own words in Surah Nuh. Nuh told his people: "Indeed, I am a clear warner to you — worship Allah, fear Him, and obey me. Allah will forgive you of your sins and delay you for a specified term" (Qur'an 71:2–4).

native illustration · courtyard
nuh/the-call/p3

But the leaders of the people rejected him. They said: "We do not see you as anything but a man like us" (Qur'an 11:27), and they accused him of following the weak and the poor. They could not see that faith has no rich or poor — it belongs to the heart.

native illustration · rockGorge
nuh/the-ark-command/cover

Build the Ark

native illustration · sandyBasin
nuh/the-ark-command/p0

After centuries of patient calling, Allah revealed to Nuh: "None of your people will believe except those who have already believed. So do not be grieved by what they have been doing" (Qur'an 11:36). And then came the great command: build a ship under Allah's care and guidance (Qur'an 11:37–38).

native illustration · roofTerrace
nuh/the-ark-command/p1

So Nuh began to build the ark — a huge ship on dry land, far from any sea. His people walked past and laughed: "Are you building a ship here?" they joked. But Nuh answered them with calm and kept on working (Qur'an 11:38–39).

painting · gardenWall
nuh/the-ark-command/p2

The mockers did not know that the believers who laugh last are the ones who trust Allah. The ark grew plank by plank, while Nuh's heart stayed firm. He was preparing the way of safety for those who would believe.

native illustration · roundWindow
nuh/the-flood-comes/cover

The Flood Comes

native illustration · canopy
nuh/the-flood-comes/p0

Then the day of the flood arrived. Allah told Nuh: "Carry in it two of every kind, along with your family — except those already doomed — and those who have believed" (Qur'an 11:40). So the believers came aboard, a small group of the faithful, and with them the animals, two by two.

native illustration · archway
nuh/the-flood-comes/p1

When everyone was safe inside, the skies opened with heavy rain and the earth burst forth with springs. The Qur'an says: "And the flood-water carried them, while they were in a ship under Our eyes" (Qur'an 54:13–14). The waters rose and rose until they covered everything.

native illustration · cliffLedge
nuh/the-flood-comes/p2

Nuh had called his son before the flood and begged him to come aboard. But the son refused and said he would climb a mountain to escape. Nuh called out: "There is no protector today from the decree of Allah" (Qur'an 11:43) — and the wave came between them, and the son drowned.

native illustration · alcove
nuh/the-flood-comes/p3

Then Nuh called to his Lord with the softest heart: "My Lord, indeed my son is of my family" (Qur'an 11:45). And Allah taught him a gentle truth: "O Nuh, indeed he is not of your family. Indeed, he is of unrighteous conduct. So ask not of Me that of which you have no knowledge" (Qur'an 11:46).

native illustration · wovenMat
nuh/the-landing/cover

The Ark Rests on the Mountain

native illustration · shelf
nuh/the-landing/p0

At last the flood began to calm. The Qur'an says: "It was said: O earth, swallow your water, and O sky, cease. And the water subsided, and the matter was concluded, and the ship settled upon al-Judi" (Qur'an 11:44). The believers looked out and saw land again.

native illustration · windingPath
nuh/the-landing/p1

Nuh and the believers stepped out onto the blessed ground, grateful and safe. The Qur'an records Nuh's prayer: "My Lord, let me land at a blessed landing place, and You are the best of those who bring to land" (Qur'an 23:29).

native illustration · raisedDais
nuh/the-landing/p2

From that day, the family of Nuh began the human family again. Allah says: "We saved him and his family from the great distress, and We made his descendants those who remain" (Qur'an 37:75–77).

native illustration · riverBend
nuh/the-landing/p3

The ark stayed as a sign, so that the children of the world would remember: when the flood comes, the ship of trust in Allah sails. Nuh's story shows us that Allah never leaves His believers without a way of escape.

native illustration · gateway
nuh/the-lesson/cover

The Lesson of the Ark

native illustration · clothBackdrop
nuh/the-lesson/p0

Nuh is one of the greatest examples of patience in the whole Qur'an. For 950 years he never stopped calling, never stopped hoping, never stopped loving his people even when they mocked him.

native illustration · hillTown
nuh/the-lesson/p1

The flood was not the end of Nuh's story — it was the beginning of a new one. Allah honored Nuh among the prophets with the best of praises: "Peace be upon Nuh among the worlds. Indeed, We thus reward the doers of good" (Qur'an 37:79–80).

native illustration · recess
nuh/the-lesson/p2

Every child who reads Nuh's story can learn the most beautiful lesson: keep doing what is right, even if nobody listens. And when trouble comes like a flood, put your trust in Allah — He will always make a way.

native illustration · openValley
hud/the-proud-people/cover

The People of 'Ad

native illustration · sandyBasin
hud/the-proud-people/p0

Long after the time of Nuh, a strong nation grew on the earth — the people of 'Ad. Allah had blessed them with beauty, strength and great riches. The Qur'an says: "Have you not considered how your Lord dealt with 'Ad, whose pillars were never matched in the lands?" (Qur'an 89:6–8).

native illustration · roofTerrace
hud/the-proud-people/p1

The people of 'Ad were famous for being tall and strong. No army could defeat them, and they thought their power would last forever. But they used that power with cruelty and pride, bowing to idols made of stone instead of Him who created them.

native illustration · gardenWall
hud/the-proud-people/p2

Allah does not destroy a nation just for being strong — He looks at what is inside the heart. The 'Ad had forgotten that every strength on earth borrows its power from Allah, and that He alone is the Ever-Living, the One Who holds all truth.

native illustration · roundWindow
hud/hud-calls/cover

Hud Calls to One God

native illustration · canopy
hud/hud-calls/p0

Allah chose Hud from among the 'Ad and sent him as a messenger to his own people. Hud stood before them with a gentle heart and said: "O my people, worship Allah — you have no god besides Him" (Qur'an 11:50).

native illustration · archway
hud/hud-calls/p1

Hud reminded them of the One who gave them tall bodies, wide valleys and pouring rain. "Seek forgiveness from your Lord and turn to Him in repentance; He will send rain upon you in abundance and add strength to your strength" (Qur'an 11:52).

native illustration · cliffLedge
hud/hud-calls/p2

But the leaders refused and answered with pride: "O Hud, you have brought us no clear proof, and we will not leave our gods at your word" (Qur'an 11:53). Their idols — statues of stone carved by their own hands — were dearer to them than the One who made them.

native illustration · alcove
hud/the-challenge/cover

They Dare the Punishment

native illustration · wovenMat
hud/the-challenge/p0

At last the people of 'Ad said: "Bring us the punishment you warn us about, if you are telling the truth" (Qur'an 7:70). Their hearts were so hard and their pride so great that they dared the command of Allah to come.

native illustration · shelf
hud/the-challenge/p1

Hud answered gently and firmly: "I have placed my trust in Allah — so plot against me, all of you, and give me no respite" (Qur'an 11:55). His heart was calm because he knew the One who holds every destiny.

native illustration · windingPath
hud/the-challenge/p2

Facing the whole town, Hud's courage did not shake. He kept calling them with mercy: "There is no creature but that its forelock is held by my Lord — indeed my Lord is upon a straight path" (Qur'an 11:56).

native illustration · raisedDais
hud/the-wind/cover

The Punishing Wind

native illustration · riverBend
hud/the-wind/p0

Then the punishment arrived — one nobody expected. The people of 'Ad looked up and saw a dark cloud coming over their valleys. They rejoiced: "This is a cloud that will bring us rain!" But Allah said: "It is what you wished to hasten — a wind in which is a painful torment" (Qur'an 46:24).

native illustration · gateway
hud/the-wind/p1

Instead of rain, the cloud carried a furious, freezing, roaring wind. Allah said: "A wind that destroyed everything by the command of its Lord — it blew for seven nights and eight days" (Qur'an 69:7). Stones, towers, bodies — nothing could stand against it.

native illustration · clothBackdrop
hud/the-wind/p2

All that looked so great — power, wealth, palaces — turned to dust in days. The Qur'an sums up: "So those people of 'Ad became dead leaves of destruction" — and the ruins became a lesson for every generation that thinks strength is forever.

native illustration · hillTown
hud/the-survivors/cover

The Believers Were Saved

native illustration · recess
hud/the-survivors/p0

Though the wind destroyed the town, the story does not end in ruin. Allah tells us: "Yet when Our command came, We saved Hud and those who believed with him, by a mercy from Us" (Qur'an 11:58).

native illustration · openValley
hud/the-survivors/p1

The believers left the dead valley alive and unhurt — a powerful hand reached through the storm and pulled them to safety. The example of Hud is repeated in the Qur'an with this promise: those who hold on to Allah are never abandoned.

native illustration · sideStreet
hud/the-survivors/p2

So for every proud heart, the wind of 'Ad is a whisper: don't depend on your strength for even one hour; the Giver of strength is the only One who stands forever. And to every humble heart, the same wind brings good news: the ship of trust always sails.

native illustration · reedScreen
salih/the-people-of-thamud/cover

The People of Thamud

native illustration · archway
salih/the-people-of-thamud/p0

After the wind swept away the people of 'Ad, another strong tribe grew in the land — the people of Thamud. Allah gave them gardens, springs and green plains. To guide them, Allah sent their brother Salih, who said: "O my people, worship Allah — there is no god for you other than Him" (Qur'an 7:73).

native illustration · cliffLedge
salih/the-people-of-thamud/p1

The Thamud were famous for carving the mountains. They cut tall homes into the great rocks so no wind could shake them. The Qur'an reminds us of this blessing: "And remember when He made you successors after 'Ad and settled you in the earth — you take for yourselves castles on its plains and carve the mountains into homes" (Qur'an 7:74).

native illustration · alcove
salih/the-people-of-thamud/p2

The Thamud became rich and proud. They had cattle, water and strong buildings, and they said to themselves: "We are the strongest people in the land." But strength belongs only to Allah — every gift they owned came from Him.

native illustration · wovenMat
salih/the-people-of-thamud/p3

Salih reminded them with a gentle heart: "O my people, worship Allah — you have no god other than Him. He created you from the earth and settled you in it, so ask His forgiveness and turn back to Him. Indeed, my Lord is near and answers" (Qur'an 11:61).

native illustration · shelf
salih/the-people-of-thamud/p4

But the proud leaders said: "O Salih, you were a man of hope among us before this. Do you forbid us to worship what our fathers worshipped? Indeed we are in disquieting doubt about what you call us to" (Qur'an 11:62). They held tight to their idols and refused to listen.

native illustration · windingPath
salih/the-miraculous-she-camel/cover

The Miraculous She-Camel

native illustration · raisedDais
salih/the-miraculous-she-camel/p0

Then Salih told them that Allah would show them a great sign. A wonderful she-camel appeared among them — tall and strong, unlike any camel the people had ever seen. Salih said: "This she-camel of Allah is a sign for you, so let her eat in the land of Allah and do not touch her with harm" (Qur'an 7:73).

native illustration · riverBend
salih/the-miraculous-she-camel/p1

The she-camel was sent as a gift and a test. Salih made a fair agreement with the people: the camel would drink from the spring on her day, and the people would take water on their day. The Qur'an says: "She has a day to drink, and you have a day to drink, on a known schedule" (Qur'an 26:155–156).

native illustration · gateway
salih/the-miraculous-she-camel/p2

And in return for her share of the water, the camel would give them milk — enough for every family in the village. She was a living blessing in the middle of their homes, asking for nothing except a little water.

native illustration · clothBackdrop
salih/the-miraculous-she-camel/p3

Salih warned them again: "O my people, this is the she-camel of Allah, a sign for you — so leave her to eat on the land of Allah and do not harm her, or a punishment near at hand will seize you" (Qur'an 11:64).

native illustration · hillTown
salih/the-miraculous-she-camel/p4

Some of the Thamud looked at the camel and understood — this was truly from Allah. But others, who had cruelty in their hearts, waited for the chance to destroy the sign.

native illustration · recess
salih/they-kill-the-camel/cover

They Kill the Camel

native illustration · openValley
salih/they-kill-the-camel/p0

The wicked ones could not bear the sign. The Qur'an tells us: "When the most wicked of them was sent forth" (Qur'an 91:12). A cruel man from the tribe rose up to do the evil deed.

native illustration · sideStreet
salih/they-kill-the-camel/p1

He went to the she-camel and struck her legs until she fell. The Qur'an says: "But they called their companion, and he took [the sword] and hamstrung her" (Qur'an 54:29). The blessed camel that had given them water and milk lay on the ground.

native illustration · reedScreen
salih/they-kill-the-camel/p2

Now the whole tribe, not just one man, became guilty. The Qur'an says: "But they hamstrung the she-camel and insolently defied the command of their Lord, and said: O Salih, bring us what you promise us, if you are among the messengers" (Qur'an 7:77).

native illustration · terracedSlope
salih/they-kill-the-camel/p3

Allah recorded their crime: "And they denied him and killed her — so their Lord destroyed them for their sin and leveled them to the ground" (Qur'an 91:14).

native illustration · timberFrame
salih/they-kill-the-camel/p4

Salih watched with a sad heart. He had warned them over and over, but they had thrown the mercy of Allah away. Now only a few short days were left for the Thamud.

native illustration · colonnade
salih/the-warning/cover

The Warning

native illustration · sunkenCourt
salih/the-warning/p0

When the camel was killed, Salih said to the people: "Enjoy yourselves in your homes for three days — that is a promise not to be denied" (Qur'an 11:65). The punishment would not be late.

native illustration · windowSeat
salih/the-warning/p1

So the Thamud lived their last three days. The sky turned red, their faces changed, and worry sat in their hearts. They laughed, but their laughter had no joy in it.

native illustration · steppedPlatform
salih/the-warning/p2

On the third day, the command of Allah came. The earth shook beneath their feet, and a terrible blast filled the air. The Qur'an says: "So the earthquake seized them, and they lay dead in their homes, fallen to their knees" (Qur'an 7:78).

native illustration · stoneTerrace
salih/the-warning/p3

Their strength, their mountains and their palaces could not save them. The Qur'an says: "And in Thamud, when it was said to them: Enjoy yourselves for a while. But they rebelled against the command of their Lord, so the thunderbolt seized them while they were looking on" (Qur'an 51:43–44).

native illustration · mosaicFloor
salih/the-warning/p4

In one moment, the proud tribe was gone. Their carved homes stood empty, and the green valley became silent.

native illustration · palmGrove
salih/the-believers-saved/cover

Salih and the Believers Saved

native illustration · stoneBridge
salih/the-believers-saved/p0

The story does not end in sadness for everyone. The Qur'an says: "And when Our command came, We saved Salih and those who believed with him, by a mercy from Us, from the disgrace of that day" (Qur'an 11:66).

native illustration · courtyard
salih/the-believers-saved/p1

The believers walked out of the valley unhurt. Nothing could touch them, because Allah protects the believers — no one can harm the one whom Allah protects.

native illustration · rockGorge
salih/the-believers-saved/p2

Salih turned away from the ruined city and said: "O my people, I had conveyed to you the message of my Lord and advised you, but you do not like advisers" (Qur'an 7:79). He had done his duty with love and patience.

native illustration · sandyBasin
salih/the-believers-saved/p3

And the Thamud became a lesson: "So away with Thamud — they denied their Lord" (Qur'an 11:68). The strong people who mocked the sign of Allah were gone, while the humble believers lived on.

native illustration · roofTerrace
salih/the-believers-saved/p4

The story of Salih teaches every child: be grateful for the signs of Allah, treat His creatures with kindness, and put your trust in Him. The believer is always in the protection of Allah — and nothing in the world can harm that.

native illustration · gardenWall
ibrahim/searching-for-truth/cover

A Boy Looking for the Truth

native illustration · windingPath
ibrahim/searching-for-truth/p0

Prophet Ibrahim grew up in a town where the people worshipped statues made from stone. His father and the whole town around him would bow before these idols and ask them for help, believing that the stones could give them whatever they wanted.

native illustration · raisedDais
ibrahim/searching-for-truth/p1

But even as a young boy, Ibrahim's heart was not at peace with this. He knew that the true Lord should be someone very great, someone who never disappears. So he looked up at the night sky and studied the bright stars that sparkled overhead.

native illustration · riverBend
ibrahim/searching-for-truth/p2

When he saw a beautiful star he asked himself: could this be my Lord? But then the star set and disappeared, and he understood with a gentle heart that anything which can fade away can never be the Creator, who is always there.

native illustration · gateway
ibrahim/searching-for-truth/p3

He then looked at the moon, and it too rose and set. He looked at the sun, warm and brilliant, and still the evening came and the sun vanished as well. One by one the lights of the sky slipped away, and Ibrahim knew in his heart that only Allah, the One who made them all, deserves to be worshipped.

native illustration · clothBackdrop
ibrahim/searching-for-truth/p4

So, even before the people knew him as a prophet, the truth was already growing inside him like a treasure: there is no god except Allah. A young honest heart had found the straight path all on its own, being guided by its Creator.

native illustration · hillTown
ibrahim/calling-the-family/cover

Calling the Family to Faith

native illustration · recess
ibrahim/calling-the-family/p0

When Ibrahim grew up, Allah chose him as a prophet and sent him to his town. His task was gentle yet bold: to call the people, starting with his own dear family, to leave the statues and to worship Allah alone.

native illustration · openValley
ibrahim/calling-the-family/p1

He spoke with tenderness and love. He asked his father why he bowed before a stone that could neither hear nor help him, and he told him that true knowledge had come to him. But the father did not want to change and went on serving the idols.

native illustration · sideStreet
ibrahim/calling-the-family/p2

Ibrahim was patient every day. He invited the whole town with kind words, but the people had loved their idols for so long that they made fun of what he said. Still he kept calling, never harsh, never surrendering the truth for the sake of their anger.

native illustration · reedScreen
ibrahim/calling-the-family/p3

Because he loved his father so dearly, he made a promise to him and asked Allah to forgive him, hoping in his heart that his father would be guided. The Qur'an says: "And Ibrahim's praying for forgiveness for his father was only because of a promise he had made to him" (Qur'an 9:114).

native illustration · terracedSlope
ibrahim/calling-the-family/p4

From this a child can learn a lovely lesson: you can love people very deeply and still wish for them what is true and good. Even when people stay with their old ways, a gentle heart keeps hoping and keeps behaving with kindness.

native illustration · timberFrame
ibrahim/the-fire/cover

The Miracle of the Fire

native illustration · colonnade
ibrahim/the-fire/p0

The people of Ibrahim grew more and more angry, because their idols could not help them and the young messenger would not stop. So the town gathered together and its leaders chose a very cruel punishment for him.

native illustration · sunkenCourt
ibrahim/the-fire/p1

They built a huge fire, a great blazing furnace so tall that its warm light could be seen from far away. Then they put Ibrahim into the middle of the flames, hoping that the fire would finish what their words could not.

native illustration · windowSeat
ibrahim/the-fire/p2

But Ibrahim trusted his Lord completely. As the flames drew near, he simply placed his whole heart in the hand of Allah, saying that Allah alone was enough for him. His body entered the heat, but his heart was safe in the care of his Lord.

native illustration · steppedPlatform
ibrahim/the-fire/p3

And then the miracle happened: Allah told the fire to become cool and safe for Ibrahim. The great blaze turned calm and gentle around him. When the smoke cleared, Ibrahim stepped out completely safe, as if he had only rested in the shade of a garden.

native illustration · stoneTerrace
ibrahim/the-fire/p4

That was a lesson no statue could ever give: the hot fire of an entire people cannot harm a heart that truly trusts in Allah. Even fire obeys the command of the Only One who deserves to be worshipped.

native illustration · mosaicFloor
ibrahim/the-sacrifice/cover

The Test of the Sacrifice

native illustration · palmGrove
ibrahim/the-sacrifice/p0

In his old age, after many years of travel and trust, Allah blessed Ibrahim with a dear son named Ismail. The boy grew up gentle, obedient and strong, and he filled his father's heart with joy.

native illustration · stoneBridge
ibrahim/the-sacrifice/p1

Then came the most difficult test a father could ever face. Ibrahim saw in a dream that he was asked to offer his beloved son to Allah. The dreams of the prophets are true, so he knew the command was real and that no love could ever compete with the love of Allah.

native illustration · courtyard
ibrahim/the-sacrifice/p2

He talked with his son about the dream. Instead of crying or running away, Ismail answered with a peace that the whole world admires: "O my father, do as you are commanded. You will find me, if Allah wills, among the patient."

native illustration · rockGorge
ibrahim/the-sacrifice/p3

Both father and son obeyed Allah with all their hearts, and at that moment Allah held back the hand and sent a great sacrifice in the place of the son. It was a beautiful gift of mercy from the Lord, and the test was accepted.

native illustration · sandyBasin
ibrahim/the-sacrifice/p4

That is why the believers remember this event every year on Eid al-Adha, the festival of the sacrifice, when families share meat with relatives and with people in need. It is a beautiful yearly reminder that submitting to Allah brings safety and mercy in the end.

native illustration · roofTerrace
ibrahim/the-kaaba/cover

Building the Kaaba

native illustration · gardenWall
ibrahim/the-kaaba/p0

After the great test, Allah gave Ibrahim another wonderful task: to raise a House of prayer in the valley of Makkah, the very first House built for the worship of Allah alone, and a safe refuge for all who come to it.

native illustration · roundWindow
ibrahim/the-kaaba/p1

The builders of this House show the beauty of a family of faith: a father and his son, Ibrahim and Ismail together. They worked hand in hand, raising stone upon stone, and their hearts were full of dua as the wall grew taller.

native illustration · canopy
ibrahim/the-kaaba/p2

When the two of them raised the foundations, they prayed together from the heart: "Our Lord, accept this from us. Truly You are the All-Hearing, the All-Knowing" (Qur'an 2:127). They did not build it to be praised by people; they built it so that Allah would be pleased.

native illustration · archway
ibrahim/the-kaaba/p3

They also asked that among their children would grow a nation that submits to the one Creator, and that Allah would show them the ways of worship and turn toward them out of mercy. This part of their prayer has indeed been accepted to this very day.

native illustration · cliffLedge
ibrahim/the-kaaba/p4

That House is the sacred Kaaba that still stands in Makkah. Whenever a child sees the picture of the Kaaba, they can remember: this blessed building was raised by the hands of a great prophet and his obedient son, and Allah blessed their work forever.

native illustration · alcove
ibrahim/friend-of-allah/cover

The Friend of Allah

native illustration · wovenMat
ibrahim/friend-of-allah/p0

Through fire and thirst, through travel and trial, and through the hardest test of offering his own dear son, Ibrahim never left the path. And for that pure devotion, Allah honored him with a title of the sublime greatness: the close friend of Allah.

native illustration · shelf
ibrahim/friend-of-allah/p1

The beloved Prophet Muhammad peace be upon him told us that Prophet Ibrahim, may peace be upon him, is Al-Khalil of the Most Merciful — that means the close, intimate friend. It is a risen rank given to this great prophet, and his memory is honored by the whole family of believers.

native illustration · windingPath
ibrahim/friend-of-allah/p2

Allah did not take Ibrahim as a friend because he was rich or powerful. He chose him because of the pure heart of tawhid inside him — the heart that trusts once, and trusts again, in fire, in the empty desert, and in the moment of the sacrifice. Nothing could shake him back.

native illustration · raisedDais
ibrahim/friend-of-allah/p3

So every child who loves Ibrahim can carry a small part of this gift: when days become hard or unfair, we can also simply say, "Allah is sufficient for me, and He is the best of trustees." A heart that belongs to Allah is never really alone.

native illustration · riverBend
ibrahim/friend-of-allah/p4

Such is the whole lesson of the prophet of faith: trust Allah with a certain heart, in the little moments on the biggest tests. And whenever the fire of a difficulty or the trial of a decision looks too big, we remember the friend of the Merciful and call the same Lord he called.

native illustration · gateway
lut/the-soft-call/cover

The Soft Call to the Sinful Town

native illustration · hillTown
lut/the-soft-call/p0

Long ago, there was a town called Sodom. The people of this town had started a very bad deed that no one on earth had done before them. Allah is the Most Merciful, so He did not leave them alone. He sent them a prophet from their own people — a kind and gentle man called Lut.

native illustration · recess
lut/the-soft-call/p1

Lut spoke to his people very gently and explained: "What you are doing is a grave sin. No one in the whole world has done it before you. Come back to Allah and to a clean and pure life, so that your hearts may find peace." He called them morning and evening, with patience and love.

native illustration · openValley
lut/the-soft-call/p2

Some people of the town listened to Lut and turned away from the wrong deed. But most of them did not accept. They mocked the prophet and said: "You are old-fashioned! We will keep living the way we like." And they continued their wicked habit as before.

native illustration · sideStreet
lut/the-soft-call/p3

The wise words of the prophet did not change the townspeople, and the streets of Sodom became unsafe. Even travelers and guests were not treated kindly anymore. Lut's heart was heavy, and he asked Allah again and again: "O Allah, guide my people, for they do not know."

native illustration · reedScreen
lut/the-soft-call/p4

But Lut did not know that in that very time, Allah, the Most Merciful, was preparing something special. His honored angels were already on their way to the town of Sodom, carrying two gifts: safety for the believers, and a final warning for the people who kept refusing the truth.

native illustration · terracedSlope
lut/the-guests/cover

The Guests Who Were Angels

native illustration · timberFrame
lut/the-guests/p0

One evening, handsome young travelers reached the gates of Sodom. They were clean and tired from the long road, and they had nowhere to stay. Lut saw them from afar and became worried, because he knew how the people of his town treated strangers.

native illustration · colonnade
lut/the-guests/p1

Lut hurried to the travelers and said softly: "Please come with me, you are my guests tonight. The people of this town are not kind to strangers, but in my home you will be safe." He brought them home, gave them food and a place to rest — for the prophets always welcome guests with open arms.

native illustration · sunkenCourt
lut/the-guests/p2

But Lut's heart was still heavy. He knew his people, and he feared for his guests. The Qur'an tells us about that moment: "And when Our messengers came to Lut, he was distressed on their account and grieved, and he said: 'This is a very hard day!'" (Qur'an 11:77).

native illustration · windowSeat
lut/the-guests/p3

When the angels saw the worry in Lut's eyes, they comforted him with a secret: they were not ordinary travelers. They were the messengers of Allah, sent to save Lut and to punish the evil town. But they told him: "Keep this secret hidden until the night comes, and do not worry — Allah has already planned everything."

native illustration · steppedPlatform
lut/the-guests/p4

That night, the people of Sodom learned that strangers had come to Lut's house. Like a storm, they rushed to his door with bad intentions. Lut stood between them and his guests and begged: "O my people, please fear Allah and do not disgrace me in front of my guests. Is there not one good man among you?"

native illustration · stoneTerrace
lut/the-threat/cover

The Threat to the Guests

native illustration · mosaicFloor
lut/the-threat/p0

The people did not listen to the noble prophet. They pressed forward and pushed against the door, shouting ugly words at the innocent guests. Lut felt helpless and wished he had strong men of his own to defend them. His voice grew tired, but he did not step back.

native illustration · palmGrove
lut/the-threat/p1

Then, at the worst moment of the night, the guests spoke. They showed their true faces and said: "O Lut, we are the messengers of your Lord! These people will never be able to harm us. Leave this town tonight with your family, and let no one look back — except your wife, for she will share the fate of the townspeople."

native illustration · stoneBridge
lut/the-threat/p2

The warning of the guests was clear, and Lut understood that the morning would bring the end of Sodom. He turned to his family and said: "We must leave now, in the darkness, before the people can stop us. Allah will take care of the rest." His believing family trusted him, and their steps were silent as they prepared to go.

native illustration · courtyard
lut/the-threat/p3

In the deepest hour of the night, Lut and his family slipped out of the town like shadows. His wife stayed behind, for she loved the wicked life of her people more than the truth. The town slept, dreaming of its evil plans, not knowing that the last night of Sodom was passing away.

native illustration · rockGorge
lut/the-threat/p4

The angels had told Lut everything before they parted: "Depart with your family in a part of the night, and let no one of you look back. For indeed, what is to befall them is near, and its morning is coming fast" (Qur'an 11:81). Lut held his family close and walked forward, trusting the promise of his Lord.

native illustration · sandyBasin
lut/the-punishment/cover

The Morning Punishment

native illustration · roofTerrace
lut/the-punishment/p0

As the first light of dawn touched the sky, the town of Sodom woke up just like any other day. But that day was not like any other day. The people of Sodom had refused the warnings again and again, and the Qur'an says: "Indeed, We sent upon them a storm of stones, except the family of Lut — We saved them before dawn" (Qur'an 54:34).

native illustration · gardenWall
lut/the-punishment/p1

The angels of Allah stretched their wings and lifted the town up high into the sky, and then they dropped it down with a mighty crash — turning it completely upside down. The Qur'an describes it: "So We turned the town upside down, and rained upon it stones of baked clay" (Qur'an 11:82).

native illustration · roundWindow
lut/the-punishment/p2

Stones of baked clay rained upon the town without stopping — stone after stone, from the sky, hitting every house and every street. Nothing could escape the punishment of Allah. The Qur'an tells us: "So when Our command came, We turned it upside down and rained upon it stones of clay, one after another" (Qur'an 11:82–83).

native illustration · canopy
lut/the-punishment/p3

And what about Lut? Allah's mercy was faster than the punishment. The Qur'an says: "And We saved Lut and his family, except his wife — she was of those who stayed behind" (Qur'an 7:83). Lut and his believing family had walked out in the night, and they watched the town of their people disappear from behind them, just as the angels had promised.

native illustration · archway
lut/the-punishment/p4

The town that had refused the truth for so long became a lesson written in the earth. The stones of baked clay that fell that morning were a sign for the people who came after. And the Qur'an gives us the most beautiful promise: "And indeed, they are not far from the wrongdoers" (Qur'an 11:83) — meaning that the example of Sodom stands for all time, so that every wrongdoer may be warned.

native illustration · cliffLedge
lut/the-lesson/cover

The Lesson That Never Grows Old

native illustration · alcove
lut/the-lesson/p0

The story of Lut is not just a story from long ago — it is a lesson that Allah placed in the Qur'an for every child and every grown-up, until the end of time. The Qur'an says about the stones of Sodom: "Marked in the sight of your Lord — and they are not far from the wrongdoers" (Qur'an 11:83).

native illustration · wovenMat
lut/the-lesson/p1

The first lesson of this story is: never do something just because others do it. The people of Sodom did a sin that no one had done before them, and they paid a heavy price. A child who follows the crowd into wrong is like a traveler who follows the wrong road — every step takes him further from home.

native illustration · shelf
lut/the-lesson/p2

The second lesson is: listen to good advice, even when it comes from someone younger or simpler than you. Lut was one of the kindest people of his town, and he wished nothing but good for his people. But they refused to listen, and their ears stayed closed until the morning of stones came.

native illustration · windingPath
lut/the-lesson/p3

The third lesson is the most beautiful one: no matter how dark the world becomes, the mercy of Allah always finds a way. Lut was saved because he believed and he obeyed, even when everyone around him was lost. And his family was saved with him, because they stood by the truth. Allah always keeps His promise to the believers.

native illustration · raisedDais
lut/the-lesson/p4

So whenever you hear the name Lut, remember three things: obey Allah and His prophets, never follow others into sin, and always believe that Allah's mercy is bigger than any mistake — if you turn back to Him in time. The town of Sodom is gone, but its lesson is alive, and it is written in your heart so that you may walk the clean path all your life.

native illustration · riverBend
ismail/a-dear-son/cover

A Dear Son Is Born

native illustration · terracedSlope
ismail/a-dear-son/p0

Ibrahim was a prophet who loved Allah with all his heart, and on his lips there always lived one special prayer. For long years he had been telling people to worship only one God, but still he had no child of his own. So he raised his hands and said: "My Lord, grant me a righteous son" (Qur'an 37:100).

native illustration · timberFrame
ismail/a-dear-son/p1

A father's prayer shows what lives inside his heart. Ibrahim did not ask for gold or for a great kingdom — he asked for a good son, one who would follow Allah's true religion and spread it to the world. He wanted a child who would worship Allah alone and teach others to do the same.

native illustration · colonnade
ismail/a-dear-son/p2

Sometimes Allah waits before answering a prayer, because what He gives at the perfect time is the sweetest gift. And so it was with Ibrahim. After years of waiting and praying, a dear baby boy was born to his household, and he was named Ismail.

native illustration · sunkenCourt
ismail/a-dear-son/p3

Ismail means 'Allah listens' — and indeed, Allah had listened to every word of his father's prayer. The little boy was a blessing, but his story was just beginning. A far journey and a great test were waiting for this little baby.

native illustration · windowSeat
ismail/a-dear-son/p4

A child is a trust from Allah, and Ibrahim prayed for his son with the same prayer the prophets always made. He asked Allah to send a messenger from his descendants who would recite His verses and teach the Book and wisdom (Qur'an 2:129). That prayer was kept for a son of this very baby.

native illustration · steppedPlatform
ismail/the-barren-valley/cover

Settled in the Barren Valley

native illustration · stoneTerrace
ismail/the-barren-valley/p0

When Ismail was still a little baby, Allah commanded Ibrahim to take him and his mother Hajar to a faraway valley. It was the valley of Makkah — a dry, empty land where there were no trees, no water, and no people. Only golden sand stretched in every direction.

native illustration · mosaicFloor
ismail/the-barren-valley/p1

Ibrahim obeyed his Lord without asking questions, because he knew that Allah's plan is always the most beautiful. He left Hajar and the little baby in the valley with a small skin of water and some dates. Then, with a heart heavy but trusting, he began to walk away.

native illustration · palmGrove
ismail/the-barren-valley/p2

Hajar ran after him and asked: "Ibrahim, where are you leaving us?" But he did not turn around. She asked again, and again he walked on. The third time she asked: "Did Allah command you to do this?" Ibrahim said yes. Then Hajar smiled with peace and said: "Then Allah will not let us be lost."

native illustration · stoneBridge
ismail/the-barren-valley/p3

The mother and the baby stayed in that lonely valley, and Ibrahim raised his hands to his Lord. He prayed: "Our Lord, I have settled some of my descendants in an uncultivated valley near Your Sacred House, our Lord, that they may establish prayer" (Qur'an 14:37).

native illustration · courtyard
ismail/the-barren-valley/p4

It is not easy for a father to leave his baby in an empty valley. But Ibrahim knew that what looks empty to us is full of Allah's mercy. The barren sand was not an ending — it was a beginning. And in that lonely place, Allah was about to write one of the most wonderful stories ever told.

native illustration · rockGorge
ismail/the-well-of-zamzam/cover

The Well of Zamzam

native illustration · sandyBasin
ismail/the-well-of-zamzam/p0

The small skin of water soon ran out, and the dates were finished. In the burning heat, little Ismail became thirsty and began to cry. Hajar looked around in panic — there was no water anywhere, and no help in sight. But a mother's love does not give up.

native illustration · roofTerrace
ismail/the-well-of-zamzam/p1

Hajar ran up the nearest hill, called Safa, and looked far into the desert for water or a passing caravan. She saw nothing. So she ran down to the valley and up another hill, called Marwah, and looked again. Nothing. She ran between the two hills seven times, searching with all her heart.

native illustration · gardenWall
ismail/the-well-of-zamzam/p2

Then, when hope seemed so thin, Hajar heard a voice — the sound of water. She looked and saw the angel Jibreel standing beside Ismail, and with his wing he struck the ground. Out of the dry sand, Allah's mercy gushed up as a sweet spring of water. Zamzam had begun to flow.

native illustration · roundWindow
ismail/the-well-of-zamzam/p3

Hajar rushed to the water and gathered it with her hands, and as she collected it, she said over and over: "Zam, zam!" — 'Gather, gather!' The water was plentiful and never ran dry, and the thirsty baby drank until he was happy again. The mother and child were saved by Allah's mercy.

native illustration · canopy
ismail/the-well-of-zamzam/p4

Allah made the running of Hajar between Safa and Marwah a sign for all people: "Indeed, Safa and Marwah are among the symbols of Allah" (Qur'an 2:158). Today pilgrims still walk the same path with her steps, and the water of Zamzam still flows for everyone who comes to Makkah.

native illustration · archway
ismail/the-dream-of-sacrifice/cover

The True Dream of Sacrifice

native illustration · cliffLedge
ismail/the-dream-of-sacrifice/p0

Years passed, and the little baby grew into a fine boy who walked and ran beside his father. Ismail was the joy of Ibrahim's life — a loving, obedient, and strong son. And then came the hardest test a father and son could ever face.

native illustration · alcove
ismail/the-dream-of-sacrifice/p1

Ibrahim saw a dream — a true dream of the prophets — that he was sacrificing his beloved son for the sake of Allah. So the gentle father went to his son and told him plainly: "O my son, indeed I have seen in a dream that I sacrifice you" (Qur'an 37:102).

native illustration · wovenMat
ismail/the-dream-of-sacrifice/p2

How did the boy answer? With a heart full of faith, Ismail said: "O my father, do what you are commanded; you will find me, if Allah wills, patient" (Qur'an 37:102). The child taught the whole world a lesson of obedience — he did not run, he did not cry, he simply trusted Allah.

native illustration · shelf
ismail/the-dream-of-sacrifice/p3

Father and son walked together, both obedient to the command of Allah. But just as the moment of sacrifice was at hand, a voice called from Allah: "Indeed, this was a clear trial... And We ransomed him with a great sacrifice" (Qur'an 37:106–107). A beautiful ram came in place of Ismail, and both father and son had passed the greatest test of love.

native illustration · windingPath
ismail/the-dream-of-sacrifice/p4

Allah never wanted the blood — He wanted to see a heart ready to give everything for Him. Ibrahim proved his love, and Ismail proved his patience, and both became known forever as the friends of Allah. Their story teaches us that when we trust Allah with our dearest things, He gives us back something even better.

native illustration · raisedDais
ismail/building-the-kaaba/cover

Building the Kaaba

native illustration · riverBend
ismail/building-the-kaaba/p0

After the great test, Allah gave Ibrahim and Ismail a beautiful and noble task: to raise the foundations of the Sacred House — the Kaaba — in the valley of Makkah. The very valley where Hajar and baby Ismail had been left was now to become the heart of the world.

native illustration · gateway
ismail/building-the-kaaba/p1

Father and son worked side by side, raising the walls stone by stone. The Qur'an tells us: "And when Ibrahim raised the foundations of the House with Ismail" (Qur'an 2:127). Ismail carried the stones and his father placed them, and every stone was set with love and prayers.

native illustration · clothBackdrop
ismail/building-the-kaaba/p2

As the walls rose higher and higher, the two builders looked up to the sky and prayed from their hearts: "Our Lord, accept this from us. Indeed, You are the Hearing, the Knowing" (Qur'an 2:127). They were not building for fame — they were building for Allah, and they wanted Allah to accept their work.

native illustration · hillTown
ismail/building-the-kaaba/p3

And then they made another beautiful prayer together: "Our Lord, make us both Muslims to You, and from our descendants a Muslim nation, and show us our rites of worship" (Qur'an 2:128). They prayed that the House would always be full of people worshipping the one God.

native illustration · recess
ismail/building-the-kaaba/p4

The prayer was heard. Today, Muslims from every corner of the world turn their faces toward this very House five times a day in prayer. The Kaaba that a father and his young son built together stands tall in Makkah — a sign of love, faith, and the prayers of two hearts.

native illustration · openValley
ismail/a-prophet-of-promise/cover

A Prophet of Promise

native illustration · sideStreet
ismail/a-prophet-of-promise/p0

Ismail grew up in the valley of Makkah, a young man of truth and gentleness. Allah chose him as a prophet to his people, and the Qur'an remembers him with a very special praise: "Indeed, he was true to his promise, and he was a messenger and a prophet" (Qur'an 19:54).

native illustration · reedScreen
ismail/a-prophet-of-promise/p1

What does it mean to be true to your promise? It means that when Ismail said something, he did it. When he promised, he kept his word. This is one of the most loved qualities a person can have, and Ismail carried it his whole life.

native illustration · terracedSlope
ismail/a-prophet-of-promise/p2

The Qur'an also tells us how Ismail took care of his family: "And he used to enjoin on his people prayer and zakah, and he was to his Lord pleasing" (Qur'an 19:55). He did not only pray himself — he taught his family to pray and to share with the poor.

native illustration · timberFrame
ismail/a-prophet-of-promise/p3

Ismail was the son who stayed patient in the dream of sacrifice, the brother who helped raise the Kaaba, and the prophet who never broke a promise. His life shows that even a child can be a hero of faith — by trusting Allah and keeping one's word.

native illustration · colonnade
ismail/a-prophet-of-promise/p4

And from the descendants of this very Ismail came the last and greatest prophet, Muhammad, peace be upon him. So when we look at the Kaaba today, we can remember the baby, the boy, and the prophet named Ismail — a man whose promise was kept by Allah forever.

native illustration · sunkenCourt
ishaq/glad-news/cover

Glad News of a Gentle Son

native illustration · steppedPlatform
ishaq/glad-news/p0

One evening, honored guests came to visit Ibrahim. He greeted them with peace and hurried to prepare a tasty roasted calf for them (Qur'an 11:69). When he set the food before them, they did not eat, and Ibrahim felt a little afraid — for these guests were angels sent by Allah.

painting · stoneTerrace
ishaq/glad-news/p1

The angels calmed Ibrahim with kind words. They said: "We have been sent to the people of Lut" (Qur'an 11:70). Then they gave him the most wonderful news he could ever hear: his wife was to have a long-awaited child!

native illustration · mosaicFloor
ishaq/glad-news/p2

The Qur'an tells us clearly: "We gave him glad tidings of a boy named Ishaq" (Qur'an 11:71). After all the years of waiting, Ibrahim's prayer for a child was about to be answered by Allah.

native illustration · palmGrove
ishaq/glad-news/p3

The visitors were angels, and they carried the mercy of Allah in their words. When they came to Ibrahim, they said: "Peace!" and he answered them with peace, not knowing who they were. Then they gave him glad tidings of a learned boy (Qur'an 51:25–30).

native illustration · stoneBridge
ishaq/glad-news/p4

The glad tidings were not only for the father, but for the whole house. The angels told Ibrahim: "Do not be afraid; indeed, we give you good tidings of a learned boy" (Qur'an 15:52–55). A child full of knowledge was coming to bless the family of Ibrahim.

native illustration · courtyard
ishaq/son-after-long-years/cover

A Son After Long Years

native illustration · rockGorge
ishaq/son-after-long-years/p0

When the news reached Sara, Ibrahim's wife, she could hardly believe it. The Qur'an says she laughed in surprise, and then she said: "Woe to me! Shall I bear a child while I am an old woman and my husband here is an old man? Indeed, this is an amazing thing!" (Qur'an 11:71–72).

native illustration · sandyBasin
ishaq/son-after-long-years/p1

But the angels answered her with gentle words: "Do you wonder at the decree of Allah? The mercy of Allah and His blessings are upon you, people of the house. Indeed, He is Praiseworthy, Glorious" (Qur'an 11:73). They reminded her that nothing is impossible for Allah.

native illustration · roofTerrace
ishaq/son-after-long-years/p2

It is easy to forget how powerful Allah is when we look at our own smallness. But Allah does not need young hands or strong bodies to give life — when He wants something, He only says 'Be', and it happens. Age cannot stop the mercy of Allah.

native illustration · gardenWall
ishaq/son-after-long-years/p3

And so the promise came true. Ishaq was born to the family of Ibrahim, a gentle son after long, long years of waiting. The baby who had been promised by the angels arrived, and the house of Ibrahim was filled with joy and light.

native illustration · roundWindow
ishaq/son-after-long-years/p4

The birth of Ishaq teaches every child a beautiful lesson: Allah's promises always come true, even when they seem impossible. When people gave up hope, Allah gave a child. We should never stop hoping, because nothing is hard for Allah.

native illustration · canopy
ishaq/ishaq-the-prophet/cover

Ishaq the Prophet

native illustration · archway
ishaq/ishaq-the-prophet/p0

Ishaq grew up in the blessed house of Ibrahim, learning about Allah from his father. He grew to be a righteous young man who loved Allah and followed His guidance. And Allah honored him with the greatest gift of all — prophethood.

native illustration · cliffLedge
ishaq/ishaq-the-prophet/p1

The Qur'an tells us: "And We gave him glad tidings of Ishaq, a prophet among the righteous, and We blessed him and Ishaq" (Qur'an 37:112–113). Ishaq was not only a good man — he was chosen by Allah to guide people as a prophet.

native illustration · alcove
ishaq/ishaq-the-prophet/p2

Allah speaks of the blessed chain of the family of Ibrahim: "And We gave him Ishaq and as a grandson Yaqub, and We made them all leaders guiding by Our command" (Qur'an 21:72–73). The light of prophethood passed from Ibrahim to Ishaq, and from Ishaq to Yaqub.

native illustration · wovenMat
ishaq/ishaq-the-prophet/p3

As a prophet, Ishaq called people to worship Allah alone. He taught his family and his people to pray, to be kind, and to remember their Creator. He was a gentle lamp in the valley where he lived, lighting the way with the words of Allah.

native illustration · shelf
ishaq/ishaq-the-prophet/p4

Ishaq's story shows how Allah keeps His promises. The baby who was a glad tiding became a prophet; the child who was promised grew into a leader. And his own son would carry the light even further, because Allah had blessed the family of Ibrahim.

native illustration · windingPath
ishaq/a-blessed-family/cover

A Blessed Family

native illustration · raisedDais
ishaq/a-blessed-family/p0

Allah blessed Ibrahim and Ishaq with the most beautiful of blessings. The Qur'an says: "And We blessed him and Ishaq, and among their descendants are the doers of good" (Qur'an 37:113). From this family came prophets, kings, and people who loved Allah.

native illustration · riverBend
ishaq/a-blessed-family/p1

Allah speaks of His gift to this blessed family: "And We gave him Ishaq and Yaqub and placed in his descendants prophethood and the Book, and We gave him his reward in this world, and indeed he is, in the Hereafter, among the righteous" (Qur'an 29:27).

native illustration · gateway
ishaq/a-blessed-family/p2

Think about this wonderful promise: prophethood and the Book were placed in the descendants of Ibrahim. That is why so many prophets came from his family. Ishaq was part of this golden chain, and his children carried it forward for generation after generation.

native illustration · clothBackdrop
ishaq/a-blessed-family/p3

The mercy of Allah over this house did not end with one generation. Ishaq had a son named Yaqub, and from Yaqub came the twelve tribes of Bani Isra'il. The prayer of Ibrahim was answered again and again, in every child and grandchild.

native illustration · hillTown
ishaq/a-blessed-family/p4

Ishaq's story ends with a lesson for every heart: no promise of Allah ever fails. When the angels gave glad tidings of a son, the son came. When Allah promised blessings, the blessings flowed like a river. Trust Allah, because His word is always true.

native illustration · recess
ishaq/legacy-and-lesson/cover

Legacy and Lesson

native illustration · openValley
ishaq/legacy-and-lesson/p0

After the Prophet ﷺ died, the companions carried what they had learned outward — to new towns, to their children, and to whoever asked how to pray, give, or forgive.

native illustration · sideStreet
ishaq/legacy-and-lesson/p1

Ishaq (Isaac)'s legacy is not only a story to remember. It is a way of acting that is still available: truthful speech, patient hardship, generous hands.

native illustration · reedScreen
ishaq/legacy-and-lesson/p2

Close this part with one question: which single choice from this story could you copy this week — at home, at school, or with a friend?

native illustration · terracedSlope
yaqub/son-of-ishaq/cover

Son of Ishaq, Father of a Great Family

native illustration · courtyard
yaqub/son-of-ishaq/p0

Yaqub was the son of Ishaq and the grandson of the great prophet Ibrahim. From his father he learned about Allah, and from his family he carried the light of the prophets. Allah had blessed his grandfather with Ishaq and with him, Yaqub, as a grandson (Qur'an 21:72).

native illustration · rockGorge
yaqub/son-of-ishaq/p1

Yaqub grew into a righteous man who loved Allah. He had a big family of sons, and from his twelve sons came the twelve tribes of Bani Isra'il. Because of him, the message of Allah continued to pass to a whole nation of people.

native illustration · sandyBasin
yaqub/son-of-ishaq/p2

When Yaqub was about to leave this world, he asked his sons the most important question a father can ask: "What will you worship after me?" And his sons answered with one beautiful voice: "We will worship your God and the God of your fathers — Ibrahim, Ismail and Ishaq — one God, and we are Muslims to Him" (Qur'an 2:133).

native illustration · roofTerrace
yaqub/son-of-ishaq/p3

That was the wise way of Yaqub. He knew that the most precious thing a father can leave his children is not gold but faith. So he charged his sons to hold fast to the worship of the one God, just as the Qur'an remembers: "Yaqub charged his sons" with the same belief (Qur'an 2:132).

native illustration · gardenWall
yaqub/son-of-ishaq/p4

Yaqub taught us something beautiful from the very beginning: faith is the greatest treasure a family can own. When parents teach their children to love Allah and keep His commands, they are leaving them a gift that never fades away.

native illustration · roundWindow
yaqub/the-love-of-yusuf/cover

The Love of Yusuf

native illustration · canopy
yaqub/the-love-of-yusuf/p0

Among all the children of Yaqub there was one son who carried a very special light — his name was Yusuf. Yaqub loved Yusuf with a deep and tender love. Yusuf was gentle and loved his father, and the father saw in him the beauty of patience and goodness.

native illustration · archway
yaqub/the-love-of-yusuf/p1

Allah blessed Yusuf with the gift of true dreams. One day Yusuf told his father: "O my father, indeed I have seen eleven stars and the sun and the moon prostrating to me" (Qur'an 12:4). Yaqub listened and knew that this beloved son was chosen for something great.

native illustration · cliffLedge
yaqub/the-love-of-yusuf/p2

Yaqub advised his son to keep the dream to himself, because he was a wise father who knew the hearts of his other sons. But the story of Yusuf teaches us not about favoritism — it teaches us about the patience of a father who never stopped trusting Allah.

native illustration · alcove
yaqub/the-love-of-yusuf/p3

The love of Yaqub for Yusuf reminds us that a father's heart is a wide ocean. He loved Yusuf dearly, but he also loved his other sons and prayed for them. When trouble came from the brothers' envy, Yaqub met it with patience, not with anger.

native illustration · wovenMat
yaqub/the-love-of-yusuf/p4

So the chapter of Yusuf's love opens the door to the greatest lesson of Yaqub's life: the lesson of sabr. When the days of sorrow came, Yaqub carried his heart with beauty and patience, and the Qur'an honors him for it.

native illustration · shelf
yaqub/the-patient-father/cover

The Patient Father

native illustration · windingPath
yaqub/the-patient-father/p0

The brothers of Yusuf took him away from his father, and when they came back without him, the heart of Yaqub broke. He cried and cried, and his eyes grew pale from weeping. But he never blamed Allah — he only turned to Him.

native illustration · raisedDais
yaqub/the-patient-father/p1

The Qur'an tells us: "He turned away from them and said: O my sorrow over Yusuf! And his eyes became white with grief, for he was suppressing his anger" (Qur'an 12:84). Yaqub held his pain inside and showed the world the beauty of patience.

native illustration · riverBend
yaqub/the-patient-father/p2

Even when the brothers came back with a sad story about the wolf, Yaqub said the words that became famous forever: "Rather, your souls have enticed you to something. So patience is most fitting" (Qur'an 12:18). He did not believe the story, but he did not break down.

native illustration · gateway
yaqub/the-patient-father/p3

When his sons came again, this time asking for help and bringing even harder news, Yaqub answered from the same deep well of faith: "I only complain of my suffering and grief to Allah, and I know from Allah that which you do not know" (Qur'an 12:86). He knew something the brothers did not know.

native illustration · clothBackdrop
yaqub/the-patient-father/p4

What did Yaqub know that the others did not? He knew that Allah's promise never fails. He knew that his dream about Yusuf had been true, and that one day the whole family would be gathered together again. That hope kept his heart alive through all the years.

native illustration · hillTown
yaqub/the-sight-returned/cover

The Sight Returned

native illustration · recess
yaqub/the-sight-returned/p0

Years passed, and Allah prepared a wonderful gift for Yaqub. When Yusuf grew up in Egypt, he sent his shirt with his brothers as a sign. The Qur'an says: "And when the bearer of good tidings came, he cast it over his face, and he returned to sight" (Qur'an 12:96).

native illustration · openValley
yaqub/the-sight-returned/p1

Allah had kept the eyes of Yaqub waiting, but the waiting was not punishment — it was mercy. The moment the shirt of Yusuf touched his face, his sight came back. Allah restored the eyes that had cried so many tears and the heart that had waited so patiently.

native illustration · sideStreet
yaqub/the-sight-returned/p2

And Yaqub turned to his sons with a gentle heart. They had wronged him and their brother, but the father knew that mercy was the way. The Qur'an says: "They said: O our father, ask forgiveness for us of our sins, indeed we have been sinners" (Qur'an 12:97).

native illustration · reedScreen
yaqub/the-sight-returned/p3

And the patient father promised them: "I will ask forgiveness for you of my Lord. Indeed, He is the Forgiving, the Merciful" (Qur'an 12:98). Yaqub did not scold them with anger. He opened his arms with forgiveness, just as the prophets always do.

native illustration · terracedSlope
yaqub/the-sight-returned/p4

And at last the whole family set out for Egypt. The Qur'an tells us: "And when they entered upon Yusuf, he took his parents to himself and said: Enter Egypt, if Allah wills, in safety" (Qur'an 12:99). The years of tears ended, and the family of Yaqub walked into Egypt together.

native illustration · timberFrame
yaqub/a-good-end/cover

A Good End for the Family

native illustration · colonnade
yaqub/a-good-end/p0

Then came the most beautiful moment of all. In Egypt, Yusuf raised his parents to the throne, and the whole family bowed in gratitude to Allah. The Qur'an says: "And he raised his parents upon the throne, and they fell to him in prostration" (Qur'an 12:100).

native illustration · sunkenCourt
yaqub/a-good-end/p1

That bow was not worship of Yusuf — it was a bow of greeting and honor, and a sign that the dream had come true. The dream that little Yusuf had seen as a child — of the sun, the moon, and the stars prostrating — was fulfilled by Allah.

native illustration · windowSeat
yaqub/a-good-end/p2

And Yusuf thanked his Lord with a prayer that every heart can learn: "My Lord, You have given me something of the kingdom and taught me the interpretation of dreams. You are my Protector in this world and in the Hereafter" (Qur'an 12:101).

native illustration · steppedPlatform
yaqub/a-good-end/p3

The long story of Yaqub ends with mercy completed. The father who never despaired saw his son again. The brother who was lost became a king's helper. And the family that was scattered was gathered together, all because Allah keeps His promise.

native illustration · stoneTerrace
yaqub/a-good-end/p4

So whenever a child feels sad or waits for something difficult, they can remember Yaqub. He cried, yes — but he never stopped hoping in Allah. His patience was rewarded, and the same mercy of Allah is waiting for every heart that never gives up.

native illustration · mosaicFloor
yusuf/the-dream/cover

A Wonderful Dream

native illustration · roundWindow
yusuf/the-dream/p0

Yusuf lived in a big, loving family with his father Prophet Yaqub and eleven brothers. He was one of the youngest, but he was gentle and truthful, and his father loved him very much. Yaqub could see something special and bright inside his little son.

painting · canopy
yusuf/the-dream/p1

One night Yusuf had a dream more wonderful than any dream he had ever had. He hurried to his father and said: “Father, I have seen eleven stars, the sun and the moon bowing down to me” (Qur’an 12:4).
