native illustration · mosaicFloor
abu-bakr/freeing-the-slaves/p3

Freeing a human being and taking care of them is one of the noblest works in Islam. A child today cannot free a servant the way Abu Bakr did, but that child can be kind, generous, and quick to help others — which is the very same caring heart praised by the Prophet ﷺ.

native illustration · palmGrove
abu-bakr/the-cave/cover

Two in the Cave

native illustration · stoneBridge
abu-bakr/the-cave/p0

When the enemies planned to harm the Prophet ﷺ, Allah told him to leave Makkah for Madeenah. In the dark of the night, Abu Bakr went with him — the two of them slipping out while the whole city slept.

native illustration · courtyard
abu-bakr/the-cave/p1

They hid in a mountain-side cave called Thawr. From inside the cave, Abu Bakr could see the enemy running toward it, closer and closer, until they reached the very mouth of the cave. “If they look down, they will see us!” he whispered.

native illustration · rockGorge
abu-bakr/the-cave/p2

The Prophet ﷺ comforted him and said: “Do not be sad; indeed Allah is with us.” A spider had covered the entrance with its web, and the enemy — never glancing below their feet — passed by without seeing the two hidden travelers.

native illustration · sandyBasin
abu-bakr/the-cave/p3

From that night, the two walked together again, safe under Allah’s protection. With faith like Abu Bakr’s, a person never walks alone; Allah is always with His believing servants.

native illustration · roofTerrace
abu-bakr/first-caliph/cover

The First Leader

native illustration · gardenWall
abu-bakr/first-caliph/p0

When the Prophet ﷺ passed away, the Muslims felt very sad, and they came together to decide who would lead them. They looked at one another, because nobody could imagine the community without the Prophet ﷺ.

native illustration · roundWindow
abu-bakr/first-caliph/p1

Then Umar (RA) stepped forward and called the believers to agree on Abu Bakr. Abu Bakr said he was not the best among them, but the people answered: “We do not know anyone better than you.” So Abu Bakr became the first caliph.

native illustration · canopy
abu-bakr/first-caliph/p2

Abu Bakr led with a gentle but firm hand. Some people refused to pay the zakah after the Prophet ﷺ had passed away, and Abu Bakr faced them bravely to keep the community of Islam whole. He also asked the companions to gather the words of the Qur’an into one safe book, so that nothing of it would ever be lost.

native illustration · archway
abu-bakr/first-caliph/p3

Abu Bakr ruled only a short while, but he kept Islam safe and complete. When he passed away, the Muslims remembered him as the truest friend of the Prophet ﷺ — the honest leader who carried Islam forward after the Messenger of Allah.

native illustration · cliffLedge
umar/conversion/cover

The Sword That Became a Shield

native illustration · reedScreen
umar/conversion/p0

Before he accepted Islam, Umar ibn al-Khattab was one of the strongest and most respected men in Makkah. He was tall, quick-tempered, and utterly fearless, and the people of the city were careful never to cross him. When the Prophet ﷺ began to call people to Allah, Umar was one of the fiercest opponents, and he meant to use all his strength to stop the new faith.

native illustration · terracedSlope
umar/conversion/p1

One day Umar became so angry that he took up his sword and walked out across Makkah, determined to confront the Prophet ﷺ himself. On the way a friend stopped him and said: "Before you go anywhere, Umar, look at your own family first." So Umar turned instead toward the house of his sister and her husband — and he learned that they had quietly accepted Islam.

native illustration · timberFrame
umar/conversion/p2

At his sister’s house, Umar heard the words of the Qur’an being read in a gentle voice. He asked to see them, and as he read he came upon a beautiful verse: "Indeed, it is I — I am Allah — there is no god except Me" (Qur’an 20:14). The words entered his heart like cool water on thirsty earth, and he wanted to hear more.

native illustration · colonnade
umar/conversion/p3

Then and there, Umar went to the Prophet ﷺ and said, plainly and sincerely, that he believed. He accepted Islam with his whole strong heart, and the Muslims of Makkah felt a new safety from that very day. A proud voice that had been raised against the truth now spoke for it.

native illustration · sunkenCourt
umar/conversion/p4

The sword that Umar had carried against the believers became a shield for them. He used the same courage and boldness to stand beside the companions and to protect the young Muslims who were weak. His story teaches every child that an honest heart can change, and that the strongest hearts belong to the truth of Allah.

native illustration · windowSeat
umar/days-of-hardship/cover

The Days of Hardship

native illustration · steppedPlatform
umar/days-of-hardship/p0

When the Muslims came from Makkah to Madinah, they left behind their homes and much of their wealth. The people of Madinah opened their doors and shared whatever they had, and the two groups became one large family. Even so, no one pretended the first months were easy.

native illustration · stoneTerrace
umar/days-of-hardship/p1

There were days when food was scarce and money was lighter than a leaf. Umar never forgot how the Prophet ﷺ would sometimes tie a stone over his own stomach to press down the hunger, living on the simplest meals the earth could give. The believers knew those hard days well — every cup of water was counted and shared.

native illustration · mosaicFloor
umar/days-of-hardship/p2

Yet nobody grumbled or lost heart. The Muhajirun, who had left Makkah, and the Ansar, who welcomed them in Madinah, worked, ate, and prayed as brothers. When one had nothing, the other gave more than what was needed, and the tie of faith grew stronger than jewels.

native illustration · palmGrove
umar/days-of-hardship/p3

The hard days taught them patience, and the patience brought fruit. They became grateful for every bite of bread and every cup of water, and their hearts stayed soft even after wealth came. That is the lesson for every child: a thankful heart turns the smallest gift into a great treasure.

native illustration · stoneBridge
umar/treaty-of-hudaybiyyah/cover

A Victory That Looked Like Peace

native illustration · courtyard
umar/treaty-of-hudaybiyyah/p0

In the sixth year of the hijrah, the Prophet ﷺ and more than a thousand companions set out for the little journey of umrah, hoping only to visit the Kaaba. They carried no swords and no war plan. But the people of Makkah would not let them enter the city, and the believers stopped at a place called Hudaybiyyah.

native illustration · rockGorge
umar/treaty-of-hudaybiyyah/p1

For many days the two sides talked, and at last a peace treaty was written down. The Muslims would return home that year and come back next year for their umrah, and for ten years no one would fight the other. To Umar, those old terms looked like giving up, and his chest was filled with sadness and doubt.

native illustration · sandyBasin
umar/treaty-of-hudaybiyyah/p2

Heavy with worry, Umar went to the Prophet ﷺ and asked: "Are we not upon the truth, and are they not upon falsehood?" The Prophet ﷺ answered gently: "I am the Messenger of Allah, and I will not go against His command." Abu Bakr gave the same calm answer, and at last Umar accepted the treaty and found peace.

native illustration · roofTerrace
umar/treaty-of-hudaybiyyah/p3

Then Allah made clear what no one saw, revealing: "Indeed, We have granted you a clear victory" (Qur’an 48:1). A victory with no battle at all. The next year, the Prophet ﷺ and the believers entered Makkah and made their umrah, exactly as the treaty promised.

native illustration · gardenWall
umar/treaty-of-hudaybiyyah/p4

With peace in time the door of Makkah opened to Islam itself, and many people entered the faith in great numbers. Umar learned — and every child can learn too — that what looks like a bitter wait can be a victory that Allah has hidden inside patience.

native illustration · roundWindow
umar/al-faruq/cover

Al-Faruq, the One Who Knew the Truth

native illustration · canopy
umar/al-faruq/p0

The believers gave Umar a proud name: al-Faruq, which means the one who sees truth apart from falsehood. Because his mind was sharp and his heart was honest, he went for the right thing and called the wrong thing wrong. That quality of his gave him praise all his life.

native illustration · archway
umar/al-faruq/p1

Umar was a commander of strict justice but also of soft kindness for the weak. He slept on the floor, ate simple food, and lived in Madinah like one of the working people, even while he protected the rights and property of all with a lion’s courage. If a leader was unfair, even a little, Umar called him back to the path of honesty.

native illustration · cliffLedge
umar/al-faruq/p2

People still remember how the Caliph Umar would wander the lanes of Madinah with a lamp at night, to be sure that no one was hungry and no one had been forgotten. The lonely and the widows and the weary travelers were never a stranger to his lamp. He guarded the city like a shepherd whose head stays alert all night.

native illustration · alcove
umar/al-faruq/p3

Allah invites the believers to this justice: "Hold firmly to justice, being witnesses for Allah, and let the hatred of a nation not cause you to stray from the good" (Qur’an 5:8). A child can follow al-Faruq in a small way: return what you found, read a true word, and be fair to the one who is smaller than you.

native illustration · wovenMat
umar/second-caliph/cover

The Second and Just Leader

native illustration · shelf
umar/second-caliph/p0

When the first caliph, Abu Bakr, became ill and his time was near, he chose the man he trusted the most to lead the Muslims after him. He chose Umar, and the whole community accepted his choice with love. So when Abu Bakr passed away, Umar became the second Caliph of the Muslims.

native illustration · windingPath
umar/second-caliph/p1

Umar ruled a wide land, yet even then he lived like the simplest of the people. His clothes were often patched, and he guarded the public treasury of the Muslims with more care than his own purse. Power did not change him; it only made his heart more careful.

native illustration · raisedDais
umar/second-caliph/p2

Under his rule the light of Islam went into Syria and Persia and far beyond. He built cities for the Muslim people, laid out roads and marketplaces, and arranged the affairs of the state. He encouraged the believers to count the years from the hijrah, the blessed journey of the Prophet ﷺ, so that time itself would praise that beginning.

native illustration · riverBend
umar/second-caliph/p3

Above everything, Umar kept his scales of fairness upright until the very end — nobody, whether rich or weak, was above the law. In every land under his rule, the weak slept in peace, for they knew the Caliph guarded their rights. That is why he remains the symbol of fairness: he was never afraid to tell the truth, and he always sided with the one being wronged.

native illustration · gateway
uthman/well-of-rumah/cover

The Well That Belonged to Everyone

native illustration · windowSeat
uthman/well-of-rumah/p0

In Madinah, fresh water could be more precious than gold. There was one sweet well called the Well of Rumah, but it belonged to a man who sold its water cup by cup, and the poor people could not drink unless they paid. One day the Prophet ﷺ stood before the companions and said: "Who will buy this well and give it freely to the Muslims?"

native illustration · steppedPlatform
uthman/well-of-rumah/p1

Uthman ibn Affan, a wealthy and generous man, agreed at once. He went to the owner and bought the well with his own money — paying every piece the man asked until the well was his. Then he opened it to all the people of Madinah, so that the rich and the poor, the guest and the stranger, could all drink from it freely.

native illustration · stoneTerrace
uthman/well-of-rumah/p2

From that day the sweet water of Rumah flowed for every traveller who was thirsty, and the gift of Uthman kept giving long after the seller had counted his coins. The Prophet ﷺ promised that such a gift would be a beloved treasure for Uthman. A gift that helps many people keeps growing in value in front of Allah.

native illustration · mosaicFloor
uthman/well-of-rumah/p3

Free water for everyone had become a shared treasure of the whole city. Uthman did not wait to be asked twice — his wealth flowed from his hand into the service of others. That is true generosity: not giving what the heart does not need, but giving quickly and quietly what someone else truly needs.

native illustration · palmGrove
uthman/the-two-lights/cover

The Shyness of Uthman

native illustration · stoneBridge
uthman/the-two-lights/p0

Uthman ibn Affan was a noble, gentle man, and the Prophet ﷺ loved him very much. He was honored to be married to one daughter of the Prophet ﷺ, and when she passed away, the Prophet ﷺ married him to another — so the people praised him as Dhun-Nurayn, "the Possessor of the Two Lights."

native illustration · courtyard
uthman/the-two-lights/p1

Uthman was famous for a beautiful quality: hayaa, the quiet, noble shyness. He spoke softly, lowered his gaze, and never moved forward with a rude or showy word. His modesty about living is a sign of a heart that loves Allah and respects the feelings of others.

native illustration · rockGorge
uthman/the-two-lights/p2

The Prophet ﷺ honored him for this, and the companions remember how the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would say that the light of Uthman was a sign of his goodness and his closeness to the truth. Shyness did not make Uthman weak — it made him gentle, careful, and loved by everyone around him.

native illustration · sandyBasin
uthman/the-two-lights/p3

Being shy in a good way means guarding the tongue and feeling the feelings of others. A child does not lose courage by being gentle; the two go together, as Uthman showed. Kind eyes, soft words, and a heart that does not wound — that was the good shyness of Uthman ibn Affan.

native illustration · roofTerrace
uthman/one-quran/cover

One Book for the Whole World

native illustration · gardenWall
uthman/one-quran/p0

The Qur’an is the Book of Allah, and it was revealed to the Prophet ﷺ and then quickly recorded by the scribes of the Prophet and memorized in the hearts of thousands. After the Prophet ﷺ passed away, the Islamic world grew, with Muslims in new lands, and sometimes they recited the same words in their own different ways of reading.

native illustration · roundWindow
uthman/one-quran/p1

A wise companion named Hudhayfah came to the Caliph Uthman one day with a worried report. The people of the different places had begun to argue over the best way of reading. He urged him: "O leader of the believers, stop the people before they quarrel over the Book of Allah like those before them."

native illustration · canopy
uthman/one-quran/p2

So Uthman gathered the scholars of the Qur’an, among them the great Zaid ibn Thabit, and commanded them to collect the words into one. They gathered all the pages together, checked them against the memory of the memorizers, and wrote out one complete, careful Book in the finest Arabic — the speech of the Prophet ﷺ.

native illustration · archway
uthman/one-quran/p3

Copies of this one Mushaf were sent to every far city of the Muslims, so that all would read from the same book and no one would lose a single letter of the Revelation. And that Book is the same one that a Muslim opens anywhere on the earth today, whether in Makkah, Madeenah, Cairo, or another home.

native illustration · cliffLedge
uthman/one-quran/p4

Because Uthman cared for the Qur’an and brought it together, the Word of Allah has never been lost. This story teaches: value the Book of Allah, learn it, and hold on to its words. And Uthman is rightly praised as the one who gathered the Qur’an into one.

native illustration · alcove
uthman/army-of-hardship/cover

The Army of the Hard Days

native illustration · wovenMat
uthman/army-of-hardship/p0

Once the Prophet ﷺ called the believers to a long and difficult expedition in the scorching heat, to the north, toward the land of Tabuk. The road was far, the weather was burning, and the believers had little to spend. It was hard, and only the ones whose hearts were strong stood up ready to go.

native illustration · shelf
uthman/army-of-hardship/p1

The Prophet ﷺ spoke of the great reward for the one who would give generously to equip this struggling army. Uthman came forward and gave with a wide open heart — camels, money, and supplies, until the whole of the expedition was ready thanks to him. He wanted no praise for these, and no return in themselves.

native illustration · windingPath
uthman/army-of-hardship/p2

The companions all saw Uthman’s gift, and the Prophet ﷺ prayed a joyful prayer for him. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ spoke warmly of the one who gives for the advantage of the Muslims, and the companions remembered that Uthman’s charity would be a treasure for him in the sight of Allah. Whatever good he did after that day, it is a tree that keeps bearing its fruit.

native illustration · raisedDais
uthman/army-of-hardship/p3

Later, Uthman brought that same spirit to the caliphate. He taught order to the armies of the growing Ummah, divided the new lands with fairness and provided for the soldiers. Strong and organized armies, generous and honest — that was the way of the Prophet ﷺ for the Islamic rule that went out beyond.

native illustration · riverBend
uthman/army-of-hardship/p4

Generosity is measured not in coins but in the readiness of the heart. Uthman equipped the army so completely that the entire team could move forward, and the hearts of the believers were filled with trust. A child can give a coin, share a snack, or welcome a new friend — that is the same generous spirit at work on their little scale.

native illustration · gateway
uthman/third-caliph/cover

The Third Leader of the Muslims

native illustration · clothBackdrop
uthman/third-caliph/p0

When the second caliph, Umar, passed away, the great companions gathered together to choose the next leader of the believers. After open and honest counsel they agreed with one heart on Uthman. So Uthman became the third Caliph, gently and humbly, the open book of his hands extended to all.

native illustration · hillTown
uthman/third-caliph/p1

Uthman carried the justice and the order laid down by Abu Bakr and Umar, and he added his own gentle and generous spirit. He enlarged the Prophet’s mosque in Madinah and planned the streets and houses of the city, while at home he lived simply and kept nothing but a quiet heart.

native illustration · recess
uthman/third-caliph/p2

During his time the light of the Islamic state reached new horizons and lands. The call of the faith crossed rivers and mountains, and the pattern of his gentle, careful leadership strengthened the kingdom that wanted peace and faith on both of them.

native illustration · openValley
uthman/third-caliph/p3

Above all, Uthman is the champion of the Qur’an: he made the Book one, and by that he held the whole Ummah together. A child today can open his own Mushaf and find the care of Uthman in it — together with a sweet well for the thirsty and an open hand for the believers. That is Uthman: the shy, the generous, the hero of the two lights and of the one Book.

native illustration · sideStreet
ali/boy-in-the-prophets-house/cover

A Boy in the Prophet’s House

native illustration · stoneBridge
ali/boy-in-the-prophets-house/p0

Long ago, Makkah had a year when the rain did not come and the ground grew hard and dry. Life was very hard for everyone, including a good uncle of the Prophet ﷺ named Abu Talib, who had many young ones to feed.

native illustration · courtyard
ali/boy-in-the-prophets-house/p1

The Prophet ﷺ always helped people, so he took his little cousin Ali into his own home. Ali grew up eating, sleeping, and learning beside the most honest man in all of Makkah.

native illustration · rockGorge
ali/boy-in-the-prophets-house/p2

From a very small age, Ali watched everything the Prophet ﷺ did. He saw how gently he spoke to the little ones, how kindly he treated his neighbors, and how he never, ever told a lie.

native illustration · sandyBasin
ali/boy-in-the-prophets-house/p3

Because of this, the Prophet ﷺ shaped the man Ali would become. Years later, everyone who knew him said the same thing: growing up in the Prophet’s house had filled his heart with light.

native illustration · roofTerrace
ali/the-first-of-the-children/cover

The First of the Children

native illustration · gardenWall
ali/the-first-of-the-children/p0

When the Prophet ﷺ began to call people to worship Allah alone, some of his dearest listeners were his own family. Among them was a boy — Ali — who was still young, standing at the edge between childhood and the story of Islam.

native illustration · roundWindow
ali/the-first-of-the-children/p1

One day the Prophet ﷺ asked his family to be the first to accept the message. Ali did not wait, and he did not ask for more signs. He already loved the Prophet ﷺ so much that his heart said “I believe” right away.

native illustration · canopy
ali/the-first-of-the-children/p2

The Seerah tells us that among the boys and girls of Makkah, Ali was the first child to accept Islam. He was only around ten years old, but his courage stood taller than many grown-ups.

native illustration · archway
ali/the-first-of-the-children/p3

Being first is not about being the biggest or the strongest. Ali showed that even a child can lead the way with a brave heart, and that loving Allah from a young heart is a very real thing.

native illustration · cliffLedge
ali/the-first-of-the-children/p4

In those very first days, the household of the Prophet ﷺ was just three people: the Prophet ﷺ, his wife Khadijah, and young Ali. Together they carried the first small light of Islam before anyone else joined.

native illustration · alcove
ali/the-brave-fighter/cover

The Brave Fighter

native illustration · wovenMat
ali/the-brave-fighter/p0

When the believers moved to Madinah, enemies from Makkah still came to fight them. In the great battlefield of Badr, Ali was still a young man, yet he stood in the lines of the fighters with a heart as steady as the strongest shield.

native illustration · shelf
ali/the-brave-fighter/p1

At Badr, a small and faithful army of Muslims faced many enemies, and Allah gave them a wonderful victory. Ali fought with all his strength, protecting his Prophet ﷺ and the believers beside him.

native illustration · windingPath
ali/the-brave-fighter/p2

Years later, when it came to the strong forts of Khaybar, the Prophet ﷺ said: “I will give the flag tomorrow to a man who loves Allah and His Messenger, and whom Allah and His Messenger love.” Everyone could hardly wait for the morning.

native illustration · raisedDais
ali/the-brave-fighter/p3

The next day, the Prophet ﷺ called for no one but Ali and placed the flag in his hand. With the flag in hand, Ali said he would first call the enemy to Islam — and only if they refused would there be a fight for the truth.

native illustration · riverBend
ali/the-brave-fighter/p4

That day Ali carried the flag as boldly as a lion, and the people began to call him the lion of Allah. All his strength, he always said, belonged not to himself but to Allah.

native illustration · gateway
ali/the-prophets-praise/cover

Deep Praise From the Prophet ﷺ

native illustration · clothBackdrop
ali/the-prophets-praise/p0

The Prophet ﷺ loved Ali very much and praised him on many special days. During a very long journey, Ali had to stay behind in Madinah for a while, and some troublemakers whispered: “Look, he was left behind!”

native illustration · hillTown
ali/the-prophets-praise/p1

Ali hurried after the Prophet ﷺ to explain. The Prophet ﷺ then said to him those famous words: “Are you not satisfied to be to me like Harun to Musa?” — like Aaron, the brother and helper of the prophet Moses, peace be upon them.

native illustration · recess
ali/the-prophets-praise/p2

And the Prophet ﷺ added the deepest of them all: “But there is no prophet after me.” For a while, Ali’s place beside the Messenger of Allah was the closest a companion could ever reach.

native illustration · openValley
ali/the-prophets-praise/p3

Makkah called the Prophet ﷺ “the trustworthy one” even before Islam, and he trusted Ali the same way. When they were together, the town knew that whatever the Prophet ﷺ said, and whatever Ali agreed to, could always be believed.

native illustration · sideStreet
ali/the-prophets-praise/p4

Such praise from the one he loved carried Ali for his whole life. It reminds us that when someone we love speaks into our hearts with kindness and trust, it can lift us higher than we ever knew we could go.

native illustration · reedScreen
ali/wise-and-fair/cover

Wise and Fair for Allah

native illustration · terracedSlope
ali/wise-and-fair/p0

Many people remember Ali only as a swordsman. But the Prophet ﷺ also trusted him as a judge and a teacher, and he sent him to a distant land to lead the people. Ali had hands that could fight, and a heart that loved fairness.

native illustration · timberFrame
ali/wise-and-fair/p1

When the Prophet ﷺ sent Ali to judge between people, he gave him this advice: listen to both sides before deciding, so that no innocent person is ever harmed. Ali carried those words like a lamp in his heart.

native illustration · colonnade
ali/wise-and-fair/p2

Everyone could see how fairly Ali decided. He never favored the rich over the poor, and he never took the side of his own family over a stranger. His wisdom flowed from his love of Allah, which made people trust him.

native illustration · sunkenCourt
ali/wise-and-fair/p3

Ali also taught his students that a sharp mind is a person’s best weapon. Strength can win a single fight, but knowledge and fairness keep peace between people for a whole lifetime.

native illustration · windowSeat
ali/wise-and-fair/p4

So Ali’s real title is not only “the Brave.” He is also “the Wise.” Fighting was just a small part of his story — the true story is a boy who grew into a man whose fairness and gentle mind made a whole generation trust him.

native illustration · steppedPlatform
khadijah/fair-merchant-of-makkah/cover

A Fair and Honest Merchant

native illustration · gardenWall
khadijah/fair-merchant-of-makkah/p0

Long before Islam, a girl named Khadijah grew up in Makkah. As she became an adult, she proved to be one of the wisest merchants in the whole city, sending her caravans to faraway lands loaded with fine goods.

native illustration · roundWindow
khadijah/fair-merchant-of-makkah/p1

She was so honest and pure in her dealings that people called her at-Tahira, “the pure one.” Even though she was very wealthy, she never cheated a single customer and she always treated her helpers with kindness.

native illustration · canopy
khadijah/fair-merchant-of-makkah/p2

When she needed a trustworthy man to lead one of her great trading journeys, everyone told her the same name: Muhammad ﷺ, the man the whole city called al-Ameen, “the trustworthy one.”

native illustration · archway
khadijah/fair-merchant-of-makkah/p3

Muhammad ﷺ led the journey with perfect honesty, and it returned with good profit. But the true treasure was something else: Khadijah saw his beautiful character with her own eyes, and her respect and love for him grew very deep.

native illustration · cliffLedge
khadijah/the-first-to-believe/cover

The First to Believe

native illustration · alcove
khadijah/the-first-to-believe/p0

One day the Prophet ﷺ returned home carrying news that would change the world: Allah had made him the Messenger for all of mankind. His dearest wife Khadijah sat beside him, and his heart needed someone to hold the secret.

native illustration · wovenMat
khadijah/the-first-to-believe/p1

Khadijah listened, and she did not hesitate for a single moment. She was the very first person in the entire world to accept Islam — before Abu Bakr, before Ali, before anyone. She said: “I bear witness that you are the Messenger of Allah.”

native illustration · shelf
khadijah/the-first-to-believe/p2

She did not need fireworks or miracles to believe. She had lived with this man for many years, and she knew with certainty that he had never told a lie in his whole life. So when he spoke the truth, her whole heart trusted him.

native illustration · windingPath
khadijah/the-first-to-believe/p3

Her quick belief was a great blessing to the Prophet ﷺ. When the city of Makkah was difficult and most people refused him, the one part of the world where he always felt believed and loved was the home of Khadijah.

native illustration · raisedDais
khadijah/the-first-to-believe/p4

The very first “yes” in the story of Islam did not come from a grand hall or a mighty king. It came quietly from the gentle heart of a wife, inside a small home — and that first yes carried the whole religion on its back.

native illustration · riverBend
khadijah/warmth-in-the-dark-room/cover

Warmth in a Dark Hour

native illustration · gateway
khadijah/warmth-in-the-dark-room/p0

At the very beginning of revelation, the Prophet ﷺ came down from the mountain cave trembling, carrying a beautiful but enormous weight. He hurried into his home saying: “Cover me! Cover me!” and Khadijah wrapped him in a blanket.

native illustration · clothBackdrop
khadijah/warmth-in-the-dark-room/p1

Then Khadijah spoke the most comforting words found anywhere in the stories of Makkah: “By Allah, He will never put you to shame. You keep your family ties, you carry the burden of others, you help the poor, and you give to the needy.”

native illustration · hillTown
khadijah/warmth-in-the-dark-room/p2

She confirmed his heart and then took him to her wise and elderly relative, a man who knew the old scriptures. He said to both of them: the same angel that came to Moses has now come to him, and he is the prophet of this people.

native illustration · recess
khadijah/warmth-in-the-dark-room/p3

From that night on, the Prophet ﷺ knew that he was not alone on this new road. The revelation fell onto his heart like heavy rain — but Khadijah was the kind roof that kept him warm and safe.

native illustration · openValley
khadijah/warmth-in-the-dark-room/p4

Every child who loves the story of the first revelation should also learn this beautiful part: when the mountain shook and the darkness closed, the Prophet ﷺ found his light in the comfort of Khadijah’s kind words.

native illustration · sideStreet
khadijah/the-year-of-sorrow/cover

The Year of Sorrow

native illustration · reedScreen
khadijah/the-year-of-sorrow/p0

For many years the Prophet ﷺ and his family carried their faith under hard trials in the city of Makkah. The enemies treated his people harshly, and the one big protector, his kind uncle Abu Talib, passed away after many years of watching over him.

native illustration · terracedSlope
khadijah/the-year-of-sorrow/p1

Then, very soon after, Khadijah’s gentle body became weak, and she returned to Allah. In one short stretch of time, the Prophet ﷺ had lost the two people who had protected and comforted him his whole life — and the people called it the “Year of Sorrow.”

native illustration · timberFrame
khadijah/the-year-of-sorrow/p2

The believers recalled that the Prophet ﷺ missed Khadijah with such a deep and long love that even years later, when her old friends came to visit, tears of memory would gather right at the edge of his eyes.

native illustration · colonnade
khadijah/the-year-of-sorrow/p3

That hardest of years taught the believers about patience. From the Prophet ﷺ they learned that even a heart filled with grief never stops trusting Allah and never stops serving the mission, and that the year of the hardest seasons still only passes in a moment.

native illustration · sunkenCourt
khadijah/the-year-of-sorrow/p4

We honor Khadijah every time we retell the early beauty of Islam. Her years helped the Prophet ﷺ stand, and her passing taught the whole world that great love does not end — it only changes where it rests.

native illustration · windowSeat
khadijah/he-never-stopped-loving-her/cover

He Never Stopped Loving Her

native illustration · steppedPlatform
khadijah/he-never-stopped-loving-her/p0

Long after Khadijah passed away, the Prophet ﷺ married again, and one of his wives, Aisha, shared a famous honest secret: even though she had never met her, she felt the loving shadow of Khadijah in their home.

native illustration · stoneTerrace
khadijah/he-never-stopped-loving-her/p1

Whenever the Prophet ﷺ cooked a sheep or received a gift, he sent part of it to her families and old friends — quietly, no fanfare — so that the memory of his first wife would never grow thirsty in the world.

native illustration · mosaicFloor
khadijah/he-never-stopped-loving-her/p2

One day he mentioned her to Aisha, and Aisha, in her loving teasing, said she wished she could be like her. The Prophet ﷺ did not scold her; he just nodded, and reminded them both that the treasure he treasured most in marriage was the love of a true and gentle heart.

native illustration · palmGrove
khadijah/he-never-stopped-loving-her/p3

Because of this, the whole world remembers him as a faithful husband. He remembered Khadijah’s kindness after she was gone, and did not let it grow cold — he kept sharing that kindness with her friends and family.

native illustration · stoneBridge
khadijah/he-never-stopped-loving-her/p4

Kindness that we remember and share, as the Prophet ﷺ shared Khadijah’s, is the kind of love that never grows tired. We can honor this precious mother of the believers by being soft, honest, and generous — just as she was.

native illustration · courtyard
bilal/steadfast-heart/cover

A Heart That Would Not Break

native illustration · alcove
bilal/steadfast-heart/p0

Bilal was a tall, strong man who was a servant in Makkah. When he accepted Islam, his owner grew very angry. The owner threw Bilal on the hot sand and placed a heavy stone on his chest, hoping Bilal would give up his faith.

native illustration · wovenMat
bilal/steadfast-heart/p1

But Bilal only repeated one word again and again: “Ahad, Ahad — One, One (Allah).” The hotter the day, the stronger his words became. The stones and the heat could hurt his body, but they could never touch his heart.

native illustration · shelf
bilal/steadfast-heart/p2

The Seerah tells us how much goodness Bilal had inside him. Even while he was hurting, he did not become angry at people. He only remembered Allah, because His love was stronger than any pain.

native illustration · windingPath
bilal/steadfast-heart/p3

Bilal’s story teaches children a very strong lesson: your heart can be worth more than your physical strength. When we hold on to Allah even through small troubles, our hearts grow as strong as the hearts of the heroes of Islam.

native illustration · raisedDais
bilal/bought-and-freed/cover

Bought and Freed by Abu Bakr

native illustration · riverBend
bilal/bought-and-freed/p0

Abu Bakr (RA) could not watch his believing brother suffer. He gave money to Bilal’s owner and freed him, so that Bilal could finally stand up, tall and free, and praise his Lord out loud without fear.

painting · gateway
bilal/bought-and-freed/p1

Bilal the student was a free man, and free same heart he had kept in confinement came out whole. He found his place beside the Prophet ﷺ, close to him night and day, loving him more and more.

native illustration · clothBackdrop
bilal/bought-and-freed/p2

Some people once said that Abu Bakr had bought Bilal for a small profit, so the Prophet ﷺ corrected them: “If you underpaid him, we would repay it; if you overpaid him, we would accept him.” And he made sure everyone knew that Abu Bakr’s payment-turing was accepted (Sahih al-Bukhari; Seerah).

native illustration · hillTown
bilal/bought-and-freed/p3

So freedom came twice for Bilal: first his body was freed, and then his voice was freed to praise. And freedom is a blessing we should always be thankful for, and use to worship Allah and help others.

native illustration · recess
bilal/first-adhan/cover

The First Voice of the Adhan

native illustration · openValley
bilal/first-adhan/p0

When the Muslims moved to Madeenah, the Prophet ﷺ wanted a special sign to call people to the prayer. One night, a friend of the Prophet saw the adhan in a dream — the beautiful words that call “Allah is Greatest, Allah is Greatest… Come to prayer!”

native illustration · sideStreet
bilal/first-adhan/p1

The Prophet ﷺ loved the idea, and told Bilal to rise and call the adhan with these words. So the first voice of the adhan ever heard in Islam belonged to Bilal, with his deep and sweet voice calling the city to the prayer.

native illustration · reedScreen
bilal/first-adhan/p2

The Prophet ﷺ said that the mu’adhins — the people who call the prayer — are the longest ones in the necks on the Day of Judgment, honored because their voices spent their whole lives calling people to Allah (Sahih Muslim).

native illustration · terracedSlope
bilal/first-adhan/p3

Every time a child hears the adhan today, they are hearing the echo of Bilal’s voice through the world. The same turn to the prayer is the one he called with his honest heart in the very first years of Islam.

native illustration · timberFrame
bilal/voice-after-the-prophet/cover

The Voice Behind the Prophet

native illustration · colonnade
bilal/voice-after-the-prophet/p0

Bilal stayed close to the Prophet ﷺ every year of his life. When the Prophet called prayer, Bilal called, and when the Messenger traveled, Bilal went with him, and his famous shoe threw the sand of Madeenah, the Prophet ﷺ heard him in Paradise and smiled.

native illustration · sunkenCourt
bilal/voice-after-the-prophet/p1

The Prophet ﷺ once told Bilal that he had heard the sound of his steps ahead of him in Paradise because of a beautiful prayer Bilal had learned. Being a companion so near the Prophet ﷺ was the biggest reward a human heart could hold.

native illustration · windowSeat
bilal/voice-after-the-prophet/p2

When the Prophet ﷺ passed away, Bilal’s voice became heavy. He asked the first caliph, Abu Bakr (RA), to let him stop calling the adhan in Madinah, because every call to prayer reminded him of the Messenger ﷺ he loved best of all.

native illustration · steppedPlatform
bilal/voice-after-the-prophet/p3

Today the name Bilal reminds Muslims of a believer who lost everything except Allah — and from that poverty Allah raised him to honor. He is a shining proof that Allah chooses His servants not by their worldly wealth, but by the purity of their hearts.

native illustration · stoneTerrace
bilal/legacy-and-lesson/cover

Legacy and Lesson

native illustration · mosaicFloor
bilal/legacy-and-lesson/p0

After the Prophet ﷺ died, the companions carried what they had learned outward — to new towns, to their children, and to whoever asked how to pray, give, or forgive.

native illustration · palmGrove
bilal/legacy-and-lesson/p1

Bilal ibn Rabah's legacy is not only a story to remember. It is a way of acting that is still available: truthful speech, patient hardship, generous hands.

native illustration · stoneBridge
bilal/legacy-and-lesson/p2
